the closure in
L^{2}[0,T]
of the linear subset
L
consisting of all maps
(s,x)\mapsto\nabla_{s}\psi(s,x)
,
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R})
. Then
F_{Z}
can be viewed as a linear functional on
L
, and by the bound (
42
), is bounded. Then, by the Riesz Representation Theorem, there exists
\bar{h}\in L^{2}_{\nabla}[0,T]
such that

(43)

\displaystyle F_{Z}(\psi)=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),(\bar{h}(s,\cdot),\nabla_{s}\psi(s,\cdot))_{s}\rangle ds=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[h(s,X_{s})\psi_{x}(s,X_{s})]ds,\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R}).

Note that actually,
L^{2}_{\nabla}
must be considered not as a class of functions, but as a set of equivalence classes of functions agreeing
\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})}
-almost surely. This is of no consequence, however, since the bound (
42
) ensures that
F_{Z}(\psi)=F_{Z}(\tilde{\psi})
if
\psi_{x}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{x}
are in the same equivalence class (see p.279 in
[
14
]
and Appendix D.5 in
[
20
]
for a more thorough treatment of the space
L^{2}_{\nabla}[0,T]
and its dual).

Consider
\tilde{h}(s,x,y)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
given by

(44)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{1}(s,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\bar{D}(x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))}[\sigma(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))+\tau_{1}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]\bar{h}(s,x)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{2}(s,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\bar{D}(x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))}\tau_{2}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\bar{h}(s,x).

We have:

(45)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{h}(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy;X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\biggr]ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{|\bar{h}(s,X_{s})|^{2}}{4|\bar{D}(x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl([\sigma(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))+\tau_{1}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+[\tau_{2}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]^{2}\biggr)\pi(dy;X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\biggr]ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(s,X_{s})|^{2}\biggr]ds<\infty.

Moreover, for
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R})
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl([\sigma(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))+\tau_{1}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]\tilde{h}_{1}(s,X_{s},y)

\displaystyle+\tau_{2}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\tilde{h}_{2}(s,X_{s},y)\biggr)\psi_{x}(s,X_{s})\pi(dy;X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\biggr]ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl([\sigma(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))+\tau_{1}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]^{2}

\displaystyle+[\tau_{2}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\Phi_{y}(X_{s},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]^{2}\biggr)\pi(dy;X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\frac{\bar{h}(s,X_{s})}{2\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\psi_{x}(s,X_{s})\biggr]ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{h}(s,X_{s})\psi_{x}(s,X_{s})\biggr]ds

\displaystyle=F_{Z}(\psi)\text{ by Equation }\eqref{eq:FZreiszrep}.

Thus,
\tilde{h}\in P^{o}(Z)
by definition. Take a sequence
\{\tilde{\psi}^{n}\}\subset L
such that
\tilde{\psi}^{n}\rightarrow\bar{h}
in
L^{2}[0,T]
. By virtue of
\tilde{\psi}^{n}\in L
, we have for each
n
, there is
\psi^{n}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R})
such that
\tilde{\psi}^{n}(s,x)=\nabla_{s}\psi^{n}(s,x)=\bar{D}(x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\psi^{n}_{x}(s,x)
.

In particular, we have
|\tilde{\psi}^{n}|_{t}^{2}\rightarrow|\bar{h}|_{t}^{2}
, so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\psi^{n}_{x}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt

and
(\tilde{\psi}^{n},\bar{h})_{t}\rightarrow|\bar{h}|_{t}^{2}
, so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\psi^{n}_{x}(t,X_{t})h(t,X_{t})\biggr]dt\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt.

Note that if
\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt=0
, we have by Equation (
45
), that the relation holds
\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{h}(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy)\biggr]ds=0
, and hence
I^{o}(Z)=0
, so the desired bound is trivial.

Assuming then that
\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[D(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt\neq 0
, we may choose a subsequence of
\{\psi^{n}_{x}\}
such that
\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\psi^{n}_{x}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt\neq 0,\forall n
. Then:

\displaystyle J(Z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\frac{\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\psi^{n}_{x}(s,X_{s})\bar{h}(s,X_{s})\biggr]ds\biggr)^{2}}{{\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\psi^{n}_{x}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt}}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[D(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

By Equation (
45
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[D(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{h}(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy;X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\biggl]ds,

so since
\tilde{h}\in P^{o}(Z)
:

\displaystyle J(Z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{h}(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy;X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\biggl]ds\geq I^{o}(Z).

∎

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
4.3
.

Proof of Proposition
4.3
.

As noted, the form of the rate function proved in Lemma
4.5
is analogous to that of Equation (4.21) in
[
14
]
. We follow the proof of Lemma 4.8 in
[
14
]
, making changes to account for the multiscale structure and the entry of
\mathcal{L}(X_{s})
rather than
Z_{s}
in the subtracted term in Equation (4.24), which comes the fact that we are looking at moderate deviations rather than large deviations. We also use the specific information about the optimal control from the proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Once again, it is sufficient to show
I^{o}=I^{DG}
, or equivalently,
J=I^{DG}
. First we show that
I^{o}=J\leq I^{DG}
. Let
Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-w})
be such that
I^{DG}(Z)<\infty
. Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\biggl\{\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\sup_{c\in\mathbb{R}}\biggl\{c\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle-c^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\frac{\biggl|\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle\biggr|^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]}

for all
t\in[0,T]
. So for any
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R})
:

\displaystyle I^{DG}(Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\frac{\biggl|\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle\biggr|^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\biggl\{\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{T}\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\psi(t,\cdot)\rangle-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\psi_{x}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt

\displaystyle=\langle Z_{T},\psi(T,\cdot)\rangle-\int_{0}^{T}\langle Z_{t},\dot{\psi}(t,\cdot)\rangle dt-\int_{0}^{T}\langle Z_{t},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}\psi(t,\cdot)\rangle dt-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\psi_{x}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt,

where in the last step we used Lemma 4.3 in
[
14
]
. Then taking the supremum over all
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R})
, we get
I^{DG}(Z)\geq J(Z)
, as desired.

Now we show that
I^{DG}\leq J=I^{o}
. Consider
Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-w})
such that
J(Z)<\infty
.

In Lemma
4.5
, we proved for
\tilde{h}
as in Equation (
44
),
\tilde{h}\in P^{o}(Z)
. We also showed:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{h}(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy)\biggr]ds\leq J(Z)=I^{o}(Z)\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy)\biggr]ds,\forall h\in P^{o}(Z),

so that in fact

(46)

\displaystyle J(Z)=I^{o}(Z)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{h}(s,X_{s},y)|^{2}\pi(dy)\biggr]ds=\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(s,X_{s})|^{2}\biggr]ds,

where in the last inequality we used Equation (
45
).

Now, by the fact that
\tilde{h}\in P^{o}(Z)
, we have by Equation (
35
) that for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
:

\displaystyle\langle Z_{t},\phi\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\langle Z_{u},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{u})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle du+\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl([\sigma(X_{u},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{u}))+\tau_{1}(X_{u},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{u}))\Phi_{y}(X_{u},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{u}))]\tilde{h}_{1}(u,X_{u},y)

\displaystyle+\tau_{2}(X_{u},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{u}))\Phi_{y}(X_{u},y,\mathcal{L}(X_{u}))\tilde{h}_{2}(u,X_{u},y)\biggr)\pi(dy;X_{u},\mathcal{L}(X_{u}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{u})\biggr]du

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\langle Z_{u},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{u})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle du+\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{h}(u,X_{u})\phi^{\prime}(X_{u})\biggr]du

where
\bar{h}
is as in Equation (
43
), so by Definition
4.1
and Lemma
8.6
,
Z
is an absolutely continuous map from
[0,T]
to
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
. Then, using Lemma
4.2
, we have for each
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
:

(47)

\displaystyle\langle\dot{Z}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{h}(t,X_{t})\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})\biggr]+\langle Z_{t},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle.

Using a density argument, we can make sure this holds simultaneously for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and Lebesgue almost every
t\in[0,T]
(see p.280 of
[
14
]
). This gives:

\displaystyle I^{DG}(Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\frac{\biggl(\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{h}(t,X_{t})\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})\biggr]\biggr)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]}dt

For any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
t\in[0,T]
such that
\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\biggl(\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{h}(t,X_{t})\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})\biggr]\biggr)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\biggl(\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1/2}\bar{h}(t,X_{t})\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{1/2}\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})\biggr]\biggr)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]

so

\displaystyle I^{DG}(Z)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))^{-1}|\bar{h}(t,X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]dt,

and by Equation (
46
) we are done.
∎

As a corollary to the above result, we also get an alternative form of the rate function in the setting without multiscale structure. This provides us with rate functions with which it is more feasible to compare the likelihood of rare events for the fluctuation process (
3
) as
N\rightarrow\infty
in the multiscale and non-multiscale setting as opposed to the variational form given in Theorem
3.2
and Corollary
3.4
. This analysis is outside the scope of this paper, but is an interesting avenue for future research.

Corollary 4.6
.

In the setting of Corollary
3.4
, assume in addition
\sigma^{2}(x,\mu)>0
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Consider
\tilde{I}^{DG}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-\rho})\rightarrow[0,+\infty]
given by :

(48)

\displaystyle\tilde{I}^{DG}(Z)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\frac{|\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\tilde{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle|^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{2}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))|\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{t})|^{2}]}dt,

if
Z(0)=0
,
Z
is absolutely continuous in the sense if Definition
4.1
, and
Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-v})
, and
I^{DG}(Z)=+\infty
otherwise. Here
\tilde{X}_{t}
is as in Corollary
3.4
,
\dot{Z}
is the time derivative of
Z
in the distribution sense from Lemma
4.2
and
\tilde{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{s})}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{S}_{-v}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{-(v+2)}
is the adjoint of
\tilde{L}_{\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{s})}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{S}_{v+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{v}
given in Corollary
3.4
(using here Lemma
8.6
).

Then
\{Z^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies a large deviation principle on the space
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-\rho})
with speed
a^{-2}(N)
and good rate function
\tilde{I}^{DG}
.

Proof.

This follows by the same proof as Proposition
4.3
, removing the dependence of the control on
y
and setting
\Phi\equiv 0
.

∎

4.2.
Examples: A Class of Aggregation-Diffusion Equations

A common form for interacting particle systems which are widely used in many settings such as in biology, ecology, social sciences, economics, molecular dynamics, and in study of spatially homogeneous granular media (see e.g.,
[
56
,
59
,
48
,
40
]
) is:

(49)

\displaystyle dX^{i,N}_{t}

\displaystyle=-V^{\prime}(X^{i,N}_{t})dt-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}W^{\prime}(X^{i,N}_{t}-X^{j,N}_{t})dt+\sigma dW^{i}_{t},\quad X^{i,N}_{0}=\eta^{x}

where
V\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a sufficiently smooth confining potential and
W\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a sufficiently smooth interaction potential. The class of systems (
49
) contains the system in the seminal paper
[
11
]
, where many mathematical aspects of a model for cooperative behavior in a bi-stable confining potential with attraction to the mean are explored. This leads us to our first example:

Example 4.7
.

Consider the system (
49
). Let
v,\rho
be as in Corollary
3.4
. Suppose
V^{\prime},W^{\prime}\in C_{b}^{\rho+2}
,
W^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\rho+2}
, and
\sigma>0
. Then
\{Z^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}=\{a(N)\sqrt{N}[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{X^{i,N}_{\cdot}}-\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{\cdot})]\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies a large deviation principle on the space
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-\rho})
with speed
a^{-2}(N)
and good rate function
\tilde{I}^{DG}
given by:

\displaystyle\tilde{I}^{DG}(Z)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[|\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\frac{|\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\tilde{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle|^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[|\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{t})|^{2}]}dt,

if
Z(0)=0
,
Z
is absolutely continuous in the sense if Definition
4.1
, and
Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-v})
, and
I^{DG}(Z)=+\infty
otherwise.

Here
\tilde{X}_{t}
satisfies:

\displaystyle d\tilde{X}_{t}

\displaystyle=-V^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{t})dt-\bar{\mathbb{E}}[W^{\prime}(x-\bar{X}_{t})]|_{x=\tilde{X}_{t}}dt+\sigma dW_{t},\quad\tilde{X}_{0}=\eta^{x}

and
\tilde{L}_{\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{s})}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{S}_{v+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{v}
acts on
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
by:

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{\mathcal{L}(\tilde{X}_{s})}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=-[V^{\prime}(x)+\mathbb{E}[W^{\prime}(x-\tilde{X}_{s})]]\phi^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x)-\mathbb{E}[W^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{s}-x)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{s})].

We are denoting by
\bar{X}_{t}
an independent copy of
\tilde{X}_{t}
on another probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, and by
\bar{\mathbb{E}}
the expectation on that space.

Proof.

Noting that
\sigma
is constant and
\frac{\delta}{\delta m}c(x,\mu)[z]=-W^{\prime}(x-z)
, the assumptions put forward in Corollary
3.4
can be directly verified. This example then immediately falls into the regime of Corollary
4.6
.
∎

In
[
61
]
, the authors make, among other modifications, a modification to
V
in Equation (
49
) so that it is a so-called rough-potential (see also
[
35
]
and
[
7
]
Section 5), by letting
V^{\epsilon}(x)=V_{1}(x)+V_{2}(x/\epsilon)
, where
V_{2}
is sufficiently smooth and periodic. The system becomes:

\displaystyle dX^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}=-[V_{1}^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})+\frac{1}{\epsilon}V_{2}^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}/\epsilon)]dt-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}W^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-X^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t})dt+\sigma dW^{i}_{t}.

Letting
Y^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}=X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}/\epsilon
, we see this is a subclass of systems of the form (
1
) with

\displaystyle f(x,y,\mu)

\displaystyle=b(x,y,\mu)=-V_{2}^{\prime}(y),\quad g(x,y,\mu)=c(x,y,\mu)=-V_{1}^{\prime}(x)-\langle\mu,W^{\prime}(x-\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle\sigma(x,y,\mu)

\displaystyle=\tau_{1}(x,y,\mu)\equiv\sigma,\quad\tau_{2}\equiv 0,

Keeping within our setting of a slow-fast system on
\mathbb{R}
, we consider a version of this system where the fast and slow dynamics are allowed to be different, and the fast system is not confined to the torus:

(50)

\displaystyle dX^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}

\displaystyle=-[V_{1}^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})+\frac{1}{\epsilon}V_{2}^{\prime}(Y^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})]dt-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}W_{1}^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-X^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t})dt+\sigma dW^{i}_{t}

\displaystyle dY^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\epsilon}[V_{3}^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})+\frac{1}{\epsilon}V_{4}^{\prime}(Y^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})]dt-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}W_{2}^{\prime}(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-X^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t})dt+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\tau_{1}dW^{i}_{t}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\tau_{2}dB^{i}_{t}

\displaystyle(X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},Y^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})

\displaystyle=(\eta^{x},\eta^{y}).

This falls into the class of systems (
1
) with

\displaystyle b(x,y,\mu)

\displaystyle=-V_{2}^{\prime}(y),\quad c(x,y,\mu)=-V_{1}^{\prime}(x)-\langle\mu,W_{1}^{\prime}(x-\cdot)\rangle,\quad\sigma(x,y,\mu)\equiv\sigma

\displaystyle f(x,y,\mu)

\displaystyle=-V_{4}^{\prime}(y),\quad g(x,y,\mu)=-V_{3}^{\prime}(x)-\langle\mu,W_{2}^{\prime}(x-\cdot)\rangle,\quad\tau_{1}(x,y,\mu)\equiv\tau_{1},\quad\tau_{2}(x,y,\mu)\equiv\tau_{2}.

Example 4.8
.

Consider the system (
50
).

Suppose
V_{4}(y)=\frac{\kappa}{2}y^{2}+\tilde{\eta}(y)
where
\kappa>0
and
\tilde{\eta}\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
is even with
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tilde{\eta}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}_{\infty}<\kappa
,
V_{1}^{\prime},V_{3}^{\prime},W_{1}^{\prime},W_{2}^{\prime}\in C_{b}^{r+2}(\mathbb{R})
,
W_{1}^{\prime},W_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{r+2}
where
r
is as in Equation (
7
),
\sigma,\tau_{2}\neq 0
,
V_{2}
is even, and
V_{2}^{\prime}
is Lipschitz continuous and
O(|y|^{1/2})
as
|y|\rightarrow\infty
.

Then
\{Z^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}=\{a(N)\sqrt{N}[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{X^{i,\epsilon,N}_{\cdot}}-\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})]\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies a large deviation principle on the space
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-r})
with speed
a^{-2}(N)
and good rate function
I^{DG}
given by:

\displaystyle I^{DG}(Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2[\sigma^{2}+2\alpha a+2\sigma\tau_{1}\tilde{\alpha}]}\int_{0}^{T}\sup_{\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{E}[|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}]\neq 0}\frac{\biggl|\langle\dot{Z}_{t}-\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{t})}Z_{t},\phi\rangle\biggr|^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\phi^{\prime}(X_{t})|^{2}\biggr]}dt

if
Z(0)=0
,
Z
is absolutely continuous in the sense if Definition
4.1
, and
Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-w})
, and
I^{DG}(Z)=+\infty
otherwise. Here
X_{t}
satisfies:

\displaystyle dX_{t}

\displaystyle=-[\tilde{\alpha}V_{3}^{\prime}(X_{t})+V_{1}^{\prime}(X_{t})]dt-\bar{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{\alpha}W_{2}^{\prime}(x-\bar{X}_{t})+W_{1}^{\prime}(x-\bar{X}_{t})]|_{x=X_{t}}dt+[\sigma^{2}+2\alpha a+2\sigma\tau_{1}\tilde{\alpha}]^{1/2}dW_{t}

\displaystyle X_{0}

\displaystyle=\eta^{x}

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi^{\prime}(y)\pi(dy),\quad\alpha=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[\Phi^{\prime}(y)]^{2}\pi(dy),\quad a=\frac{1}{2}[\tau_{1}^{2}+\tau_{2}^{2}]

and
\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{S}_{w+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{w}
acts on
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
by:

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq-[\tilde{\alpha}V_{3}^{\prime}(x)+V_{1}^{\prime}(x)+\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\alpha}W_{2}^{\prime}(x-X_{s})+W_{1}^{\prime}(x-X_{s})]]\phi^{\prime}(x)+\frac{1}{2}[\sigma^{2}+2\alpha a+2\sigma\tau_{1}\tilde{\alpha}]\phi^{\prime\prime}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}[[\tilde{\alpha}W_{2}^{\prime}(X_{s}-x)+W_{1}^{\prime}(X_{s}-x)]\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})].

Again, we are denoting by
\bar{X}_{t}
an independent copy of
X_{t}
on another probability space
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
and by
\bar{\mathbb{E}}
the expectation on that space.

Proof.

Once we show
\int_{\mathbb{R}}V_{2}^{\prime}(y)\pi(dy)=0
for
\pi
as in Equation (
20
), it follows that assumptions
A1)
-
A12)
and
A’13)
hold via Example
C.10
in the appendix. Via Remark
4.4
, we also have
\bar{D}>0,\forall x\in\mathbb{R},\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then this example is an immediate corollary of Proposition
4.3
.

We know in this setting that
\pi
admits a density of the form
\pi(y)=C\exp\left(\frac{-V_{4}(y)}{a}\right)
,
where
C
is a normalizing constant (see Equation (
76
) in the appendix). Then since
V_{2},V_{4}
are assumed even and hence
V_{2}^{\prime}\pi
is odd, the result holds.
∎

5.
Overview of the approach and formulation of the Controlled System

We use the weak convergence approach of
[
13
]
in order to prove the large deviations principle for
Z^{N}
. As discussed in Section
3
, we prove the large deviations principle via proving
Z^{N}
satisfies the Laplace principle with speed
a^{-2}(N)
and good rate function
I
given by Equation (
33
) (see, e.g.
[
13
]
Section 1.2).

The method for this is to use the variational representation from
[
2
]
to get that for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
F\in C_{b}(C([0,T];S_{-\tau}))
,
\tau\geq w
, where
w
is as in Equation (
6
),

(51)

\displaystyle-a^{2}(N)\log\mathbb{E}\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{a^{2}(N)}F(Z^{N})\biggr)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\tilde{u}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}\left(|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\right)ds+F(\tilde{Z}^{N})\biggr]

where
\{\tilde{u}^{N,k}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N},k=1,2}
are
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}
-progressively-measurable processes such that

(52)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}\left(|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\right)ds\biggr]<\infty.

One can see that in fact the results of
[
2
]
indeed imply the equality (
51
) by following an argument along the same lines as Proposition 3.3. in
[
7
]
.

This bound on the controls can be improved when proving the Laplace principle Lower Bound (
30
) to:

(53)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}\left(|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\right)ds<\infty,\mathbb{\mathbb{P}}-\text{ almost surely.}

by the argument found in Theorem 4.4 of
[
2
]
.
Here
\tilde{Z}^{N}
is given by, for
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}

(54)

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=a(N)\sqrt{N}(\langle\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle-\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{t}),\phi\rangle),\quad\text{with}\quad\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}},\quad t\in[0,T].

\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}
are solutions to:

(55)

\displaystyle d\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}=\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})+c(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})+\sigma(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(t)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\biggr]dt

\displaystyle+\sigma(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})dW^{i}_{t}

\displaystyle d\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon}f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})+g(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})+\tau_{1}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(t)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}

\displaystyle+\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(t)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\biggr]dt+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[\tau_{1}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})dW^{i}_{t}+\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})dB^{i}_{t}\biggr]

\displaystyle(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})=(\eta^{x},\eta^{y}).

We couple the controls to the joint empirical measures of the fast and slow process by defining occupation measures
\{Q^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\subset M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
in the following way: for
A,B\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
and
C\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
:

(56)

\displaystyle Q^{N}(A\times B\times C\times[0,t])

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\delta_{\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}}(A)\delta_{\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}}(B)\delta_{(\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s),\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s))}(C)ds.

The proof of the Inequalities (
30
) and (
31
) are attained by identifying limit in distribution of
(\tilde{Z}^{N},Q^{N})
as satisfying the limiting controlled Equation (
32
). This identification of the limit is the subject of Section
8
. In order to identify this limit, we first need to establish tightness of the sequence of random variables
\{(\tilde{Z}^{N},Q^{N})\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, as done in Section
7
. The proof of tightness relies on a combination of Ergodic-Type Theorems for the system of controlled interacting particles (
55
) as proved in Section
6
and on establishing rates of averaging for fully coupled McKean-Vlasov Equations, as done in Subsection
7.1
. These rates of averaging are needed do to a novel coupling argument made in the proof of tightness (see Lemma
7.6
) to the following system of IID slow-fast McKean-Vlasov Equations:

(57)

\displaystyle d\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))+c(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr]dt+\sigma(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))dW^{i}_{t}

\displaystyle d\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon}f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))+g(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr]dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[\tau_{1}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))dW^{i}_{t}+\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))dB^{i}_{t}\biggr]

\displaystyle(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{0},\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{0})

\displaystyle=(\eta^{x},\eta^{y}),

where
\bar{X}^{\epsilon}
is any particle that has common law with the
\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}
’s and
W^{i},B^{i}
are the same driving Brownian motions as in Equations (
1
) and (
55
).

We will also make use of the empirical measure on
N
of the IID slow particles from Equation (
57
):

(58)

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}\coloneqq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}}.

Remark 5.1
.

Note that these IID particles are what we get from replacing
\mu^{\epsilon,N}
by
\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon})
in Equation (
1
). Using such an auxiliary process is a traditional proof method for tightness of fluctuation processes related to empirical measures; See
[
55
]
Theorem 1/Lemma 1,
[
64
]
Section 8,
[
15
]
Section 5.1,
[
39
]
Theorem 2.4/3.1,
[
49
]
Lemma 3.2/Proposition 3.5/Section 4 for examples of this general approach.. However, a key difference here form those proofs is that the IID particles are not copies of the limiting process (
25
), but instead are copies of the process we would obtain from keeping
\epsilon>0
fixed and sending
N\rightarrow\infty
. As seen in
[
7
]
, the limit in distribution as
N\rightarrow\infty
,
\epsilon\downarrow 0
of the empirical measure
\mu^{\epsilon,N}
does not depend on the relative rates at which
\epsilon
and
N
go to their respective limits. Hence, we are able to treat each of the problems separately, and obtain a rate of convergence of
\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}
from Equation (
54
) to
\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}
from Equation (
58
) as
N\rightarrow\infty
in
L^{2}
(see Lemma
7.5
), and a rate of convergence of
\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{1,\epsilon}_{t})
from Equation (
57
) to
\mathcal{L}(X_{t})
uniformly as an element of
S_{-m}
, where
X_{t}
is as in Equation (
25
) and
m
is as in Equation (
5
). The latter is a problem of independent interest in itself, and extends the current known results on averaging for SDEs and McKean-Vlasov SDEs, which can be found in, e.g.
[
26
]
and
[
74
]
respectively. The result is contained in Subsection
7.1
as Theorem
7.2
, and its proof is the subject of the complimentary paper
[
5
]
.

6.
Ergodic-Type Theorems for the Controlled System (
55
)

In this section, we use the method of auxiliary Poisson equations to derive rates of averaging in the form of Ergodic-Type Theorems for the controlled particles (
55
). These results are used in the proof of tightness of the controlled fluctuation process, as they allow us to couple the controlled particles (
55
) to the IID slow-fast McKean-Vlasov Equations (
57
) - see Lemma
7.5
. They also allow us to identify a prelimit representation for the controlled fluctuations processes
\tilde{Z^{N}}
from Equation (
54
) (see Lemma
7.7
), which informs the controlled limit proved in Section
8
. In particular, Proposition
6.1
is necessary to handle the terms
\frac{1}{\epsilon}b
appearing in the drift of the slow particles
X^{i,N,\epsilon}
,
\tilde{X}^{i,N,\epsilon}
in Equations (
1
),(
55
). This is where the terms involving the solution
\Phi
to the Poisson Equation (
22
) in the limiting coefficients (
23
) come from. The same analysis is performed in averaging fully-coupled standard diffusions - see e.g.
[
73
]
Theorem 4 and
[
26
]
Lemma 4.4 - but here we must also account for the dependence of the coefficients on the empirical measure, and hence derivatives of
\Phi
in its measure component appear in the remainder terms. One term involving the derivative in the measure component of
\Phi
a priori seems to be
\mathcal{O}(1)
in the limit, but is seen to vanish as
N\rightarrow\infty
in Proposition
6.3
. Naturally such a term does not appear in the setting without measure dependence of the coefficients, and is unique to slow-fast interacting particle systems and slow-fast McKean-Vlasov SDEs. Thus the “doubled Poisson equation” construction (see Equation (
63
)) and the proof of Proposition
6.3
are novel to this paper and the related paper
[
5
]
. Proposition
6.4
is used to see that drift and diffusion coefficients which depend on the fast particles
\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}
from Equation (
55
) can be exchanged for those where dependence on
\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}
is replaced with integration against the invariant measure
\pi
from Equation (
20
) at a cost of
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
. This method is employed when establishing rates of stochastic homogenization in the standard (one-particle) setting in e.g.
[
30
]
Lemma 4.1,
[
43
]
Lemma B.5, and
[
26
]
Lemma 4.2. There again, our setting is different than the standard case in that we must compensate for the dependence of the empirical measure of the coefficients, which yields terms involving the derivative in the measure component of the auxiliary Poisson Equation (
64
).

Proposition 6.1
.

Consider
\psi\in C^{1,2}_{b}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})
. Under assumptions
A1)
-
A6)
, we have for any
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds-\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\gamma_{1}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+D_{1}(i)\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)ds-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dB_{s}^{i}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C[\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T+T^{2})+\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}T^{2}]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{C_{b}^{1,2}}

where here
(i)
denotes the argument
(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})
and similarly for
(j)
,
[j]
denotes the argument
\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s}
, and
\Phi
is as in (
22
). Here we recall the definitions of
\gamma_{1},D_{1}
from Equation (
23
).

Proof.

Using Lemma
C.1
to gain appropriate differentiablity of
\Phi
, letting
\Phi^{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the empirical projection of
\Phi
and applying standard Itô’s formula and Proposition
D.6
to the composition
\Phi^{N}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},(\tilde{X}^{1,\epsilon,N}_{s},...,\tilde{X}^{N,\epsilon,N}_{s}))
, we get:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds-\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\gamma_{1}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+D_{1}(i)\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)ds-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dB_{s}^{i}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds=\sum_{k=1}^{8}\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{k}

where:

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon[\Phi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{0})\psi(0,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})-\Phi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\psi(t,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})]

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{y}(i)[i]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\Phi(i)\dot{\psi}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+c(i)[\Phi_{x}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\Phi(i)\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})]+\frac{\sigma^{2}(i)}{2}[\Phi_{xx}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle\qquad+2\Phi_{x}(i)\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\Phi(i)\psi_{xx}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})]\biggr)ds

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sigma^{2}(i)}{2}[\frac{2}{N}\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[i]\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{2}{N}\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{x}(i)[i]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})]ds

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\biggl\{c(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(j)[\frac{1}{N}\partial^{2}_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j,j]+\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr\}ds

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{6}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)[\Phi_{x}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\Phi(i)\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})]dW^{i}_{t}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{j}_{s}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{7}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sigma(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)[\Phi_{x}(i)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\Phi(i)\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})]ds

\displaystyle\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{8}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\biggl\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma(j)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{j}(s)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr\}ds.

Via Lemma
B.1
, the assumed linear growth of
b
and
c
in
y
and boundedness of
\sigma
, and the assumed bound (
53
) on the controls, one can check that indeed
\tilde{\mu}^{N}_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
for each
t\in[0,T]
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and so there is no issue with the domain of
\Phi
and its derivatives being
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Then, by multiple applications of Hölder’s inequality, and using the assumed uniform in
x,\mu
polynomial growth in
y
of
\Phi
and its derivatives from Assumption
A6)
:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}T\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{y}(i)[i]|^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}T\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{y}(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}_{s})}^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2\tilde{q}_{\Phi_{y}}(1,0,0)}\biggr]\biggr)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}+|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2q_{\Phi}(0,2,0)}\biggr]\biggr)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{C^{1,2}_{b}}

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}T\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\biggl|\biggl(|\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[i]|+|\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{x}(i)[i]|\biggr)\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr](\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}T\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\biggl|\biggl(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}_{s})}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{x}(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}_{s})}\biggr)\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr](\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\epsilon^{2}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2(\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)\vee\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,1,0))}\biggr]\biggr)(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})

Here for
\tilde{B}_{1}
, we used the assumed boundedness of
\Phi
from
A6)
. For
\tilde{B}_{2}
we used the assumed polynomial growth in
y
of
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
from
A6)
and the boundedness of
\sigma
and
\tau_{1}
from
A5)
and
A1)
. For
\tilde{B}_{3}
we used the assumed polynomial growth in
y
of
\Phi_{xx}
and boundedness of
\Phi,\Phi_{x}
from
A6)
and the boundedness of
\sigma
and the linear growth in
y
of
c
from
A5)
. In
\tilde{B}_{4}
we used the assumed polynomial growth in
y
of
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
and
\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{x}
from
A6)
and the assumed boundedness of
\sigma
from
A5)
.

For
\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t)
, we bound the two terms separately. For the first, we use the assumed linear growth in
y
of
c
and polynomial growth of
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
in
y
to get:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{N}c(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}\frac{T}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})}^{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|c(j)|^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}\biggr]+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)}|\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}\biggr]\biggr)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}\biggr]+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2(\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)\vee 1)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]\biggr)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}.

For the second, we have by boundedness of
\sigma
and the assumed polynomial growth in
y
of
\partial^{2}_{\mu}\Phi
and
\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\Phi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(j)[\frac{1}{N}\partial^{2}_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j,j]+\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\epsilon^{2}CT\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N^{2}}|\partial^{2}_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j,j]|^{2}+|\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]|^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\epsilon^{2}CT\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial^{2}_{\mu}\Phi(i)[\cdot,\cdot]\right\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})}^{2}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})}^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}\biggl[1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2(\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(2,0,0)\vee\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,1))}\biggr]\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}.

For the martingale terms, by Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, the assumed boundedness of
\sigma
,
\Phi
, and
\Phi_{x}
and assumed polynomial growth in
y
of
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{6}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})+C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]|^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})+C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})+C\frac{\epsilon^{2}a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}T\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)}\biggr]\biggr)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}.

By the bound (
52
) and the assumed boundedness of
\Phi,\Phi_{x}
, we have also

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{7}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{a^{2}(N)N}CT\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}ds\biggr](\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}CT(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}).

Finally, by the assumed boundedness of
\sigma
and polynomial growth of
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
in
y
:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{8}(t)|^{2}\biggr]\leq\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{a^{2}(N)N^{3}}C\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{j}(s)||\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]|ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{a^{2}(N)N}C\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{j}(s)|^{2}ds\biggr)\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[\cdot]\right\rVert}^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})}ds\biggr)\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}T\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2\tilde{q}_{\Phi}(1,0,0)}\biggr]\biggr)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

where we use the bound (
53
) in the last step. The result follows from Lemmas
B.1
and
B.3
, using that the exponent of
|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|
in the expectation of all these bounds is less than or equal to 2 as imposed in Assumption
A6)
. Lemma
B.3
is used to handle the last term appearing in the bound of the first part of
\tilde{B}_{5}
.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

Bounding the first term in
\tilde{B}_{5}
in Proposition
6.1
is the only place where Lemma
B.3
is required in this manuscript. The proof of Lemma
B.3
is where it is required that there exists
\rho\in(0,1)
such that
a(N)\sqrt{N}\epsilon^{\rho}\rightarrow\lambda\in(0,+\infty]
. Thus, if this term can be otherwise bounded (e.g. if
c
or
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
is uniformly bounded), one can relax this technical assumption on the scaling sequence
a(N)
to
a(N)\sqrt{N}\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. Moreover,
a(N)\sqrt{N}\epsilon\rightarrow 0
is needed so that the term
\tilde{B}_{1}
in Proposition
6.1
vanishes - without this, one cannot hope to prove tightness of
\{\tilde{Z}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, as in Proposition
7.8
there would be an
\mathcal{O}(1)
term which is not uniformly continuous with respect to time. If
b\equiv 0
and hence there is no need for Proposition
6.1
, it is possible to prove tightness even when
a(N)\sqrt{N}\epsilon\rightarrow\lambda\in[0,\infty)
. Under this scaling, we expect to get a different formulation for the rate function in Theorem
3.2
when
\lambda>0
. This is an interesting avenue for future research which we do not pursue here for purposes of the presentation.

Proposition 6.3
.

In the setup of Proposition
6.1
, assume in addition
A7)
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C[\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T+T^{2})+\frac{a(N)}{N^{3/2}}T^{2}]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{C_{b}^{1,2}}.

Proof.

Recall the operator
L_{x,\mu}
from Equation (
18
). For fixed
x\in\mathbb{R},\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
, this is the generator of

(59)

\displaystyle dY^{x,\mu}_{t}=f(x,Y^{x,\mu}_{t},\mu)dt+\tau_{1}(x,Y^{x,\mu}_{t},\mu)dW_{t}+\tau_{2}(x,Y^{x,\mu}_{t},\mu)dB_{t}

for
W_{t},B_{t}
independent 1-D Brownian motions.

We introduce a new generator
L^{2}_{x,\bar{x},\mu}
parameterized by
x,\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R},\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
which acts on
\psi\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by

(60)

\displaystyle L^{2}_{x,\bar{x},\mu}\psi(y,\bar{y})

\displaystyle=f(x,y,\mu)\psi_{y}(y,\bar{y})+f(\bar{x},\bar{y},\mu)\psi_{\bar{y}}(y,\bar{y})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}[\tau_{1}^{2}(x,y,\mu)+\tau_{2}^{2}(x,y,\mu)]\psi_{yy}(y,\bar{y})+\frac{1}{2}[\tau_{1}^{2}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\mu)+\tau_{2}^{2}(\bar{x},\bar{y},\mu)]\psi_{\bar{y}\bar{y}}(y,\bar{y}).

This is the generator associated to the 2-dimensional process solving 2 independent copies of Equation (
59
) where the same parameter
\mu
enters both equations, but different
x,\bar{x}
enter each equation, i.e.

(61)

\displaystyle dY^{x,\mu}_{t}

\displaystyle=f(x,Y^{x,\mu}_{t},\mu)dt+\tau_{1}(x,Y^{x,\mu}_{t},\mu)dW_{t}+\tau_{2}(x,Y^{x,\mu}_{t},\mu)dB_{t}

\displaystyle d\bar{Y}^{\bar{x},\mu}_{t}

\displaystyle=f(\bar{x},\bar{Y}^{\bar{x},\mu}_{t},\mu)dt+\tau_{1}(\bar{x},\bar{Y}^{\bar{x},\mu}_{t},\mu)d\bar{W}_{t}+\tau_{2}(\bar{x},\bar{Y}^{\bar{x},\mu}_{t},\mu)d\bar{B}_{t}.

for
W_{t},B_{t},\bar{W}_{t},\bar{B}_{t}
independent 1-D Brownian motions.

It is easy then to see that the unique distributional solution of the adjoint equation

\displaystyle L^{2}_{x,\bar{x},\mu}\bar{\pi}(\cdot;x,\bar{x},\mu)

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bar{\pi}(dy,d\bar{y};x,\bar{x},\mu)=1,\forall x,\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R},\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})

is given by

(62)

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}(dy,d\bar{y};x,\bar{x},\mu)=\pi(dy;x,\mu)\otimes\pi(d\bar{y};\bar{x},\mu)

where
\pi
is as in Equation (
20
). We now consider
\chi(x,\bar{x},y,\bar{y},\mu)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
solving

(63)

\displaystyle L^{2}_{x,\bar{x},\mu}\chi(x,\bar{x},y,\bar{y},\mu)

\displaystyle=-b(x,y,\mu)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(\bar{x},\bar{y},\mu)[x]

\displaystyle{}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(x,\bar{x},y,\bar{y},\mu)\pi(dy;x,\mu)\pi(d\bar{y},\bar{x},\mu)

\displaystyle=0.

Note that by the centering condition, Equation (
21
), the right hand side of Equation (
63
) integrates against
\bar{\pi}
from Equation (
62
) to
0
for all
x,\bar{x},\mu
. Also, the second order coefficient in
L^{2}_{x,\bar{x},\mu}
is uniformly elliptic by virtue of Assumption
A1)
, and by virtue of Equation (
19
), there is
R_{f_{2}}>0
and
\Gamma_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x,\bar{x},\mu}(f(x,y,\mu)y+f(\bar{x},\bar{y},\mu)\bar{y})\leq-\Gamma_{2}(|y|^{2}+|\bar{y}|^{2}),\forall y,\bar{y}\text{ such that }\sqrt{y^{2}+\bar{y}^{2}}>R_{f_{2}}.

Thus indeed we have a unique solution to (
63
) by Theorem 1 in
[
72
]
(which is a classical solution by assumption).
Applying Itô’s formula to
\chi^{N}(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},(\tilde{X}^{1,\epsilon,N}_{t},...,\tilde{X}^{N,\epsilon,N}_{t}))\psi(t,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})
, where
\chi^{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the empirical projection of
\chi
and using Proposition
D.6
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{13}\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{k}(t)

where

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}[\chi(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{0})\psi(0,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})-\chi(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\psi(t,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})]

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(b(j)\chi_{x}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+b(i)\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]+g(j)\chi_{y}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+g(i)\chi_{\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sigma(j)\tau_{1}(j)\chi_{xy}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi_{\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]\biggr)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}b(k)\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[k]\psi(s,i)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{N}\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\sigma(j)\tau_{1}(j)\partial_{\mu}\chi_{y}(i,j)[j]\psi(s,i)+\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)\partial_{\mu}\chi^{2}_{\bar{y}}(i,j)[i]\psi(s,i)\biggr)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\chi(i,j)\dot{\psi}(s,i)+c(j)\chi_{x}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+c(i)\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\biggl\{c(k)\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[k]\biggr\}\psi(s,i)+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(j)\chi_{xx}(i,j)\psi(s,i)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(i)\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+2\chi_{\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)+\chi(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\biggl\{\sigma^{2}(k)\biggl[\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[k]+\frac{1}{N}\partial^{2}_{\mu}\chi^{2}(i,j)[k,k]\biggr]\biggr\}\psi(s,i)+\frac{1}{N}\sigma^{2}(j)\partial_{\mu}\chi_{x}(i,j)[j]\psi(s,i)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}\sigma^{2}(i)\biggl[\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\bar{x}}(i,j)[i]\psi(s,i)+\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[i]\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]\biggr)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{6}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(j)\chi_{y}(i,j)\psi(s,i)dW_{s}^{j}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(j)\chi_{y}(i,j)\psi(s,i)dB_{s}^{j}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\chi_{\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)dW_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\chi_{\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)dB_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{7}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(j)\chi_{x}(i,j)\psi(s,i)dW_{s}^{j}+\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]dW_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(k)\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[k]\psi(s,i)dW_{s}^{k}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{8}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\sigma(j)\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{j}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}\chi_{x}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\frac{\sigma(i)\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]\right)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{9}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\biggl\{\frac{\sigma(k)\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{k}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}\partial_{\mu}\chi^{2}(i,j)[k]\biggr\}\psi(s,i)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{10}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\left(\biggl[\frac{\tau_{1}(j)\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{j}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}+\frac{\tau_{2}(j)\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{j}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}\biggr]\chi_{y}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\biggl[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)}{\sqrt{N}a(N)}\biggr]\chi_{\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)\right)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{11}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\biggl[\chi_{x\bar{x}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi_{x}(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{12}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sigma(j)\tau_{1}(i)\chi_{x\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(j)\biggl[\chi_{\bar{x}y}(i,j)\psi(s,i)+\chi_{y}(i,j)\psi_{\bar{x}}(s,i)\biggr]\right)ds

\displaystyle\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{13}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\int_{0}^{t}\biggl[\tau_{1}(i)\tau_{1}(j)+\tau_{2}(i)\tau_{2}(j)\biggr]\chi_{y\bar{y}}(i,j)\psi(s,i)ds.

Here we have introduced the notation
\chi(i,j)
to denote
\chi(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})
,
\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[k]
to denote
\partial_{\mu}\chi(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})[\tilde{X}^{k,\epsilon,N}_{s}]
, and similarly for
\partial_{\mu}\chi(i,j)[k,k]
. We also use
\psi(s,i)
to denote
\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})
.

Using that
\sigma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},
and
g
are bounded and
A7)
on the growth of
\chi
and its derivatives, the proof that

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{12}\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{k}(t)\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T+T^{2})\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{C_{b}^{1,2}}

follows essentially in the same way as Proposition
6.1
. For example, for
\bar{B}_{2}
, we can use the assumed linear growth in
y
of
b
and boundedness of
g
and
\sigma
from
A5)
, boundedness of
\tau_{1}
from
A1)
, and boundedness of
\chi,\chi_{x},\chi_{\bar{x}},\chi_{y},\chi_{\bar{y}}
and polynomial growth in
y
of
\chi_{xy}
and
\chi_{\bar{x}\bar{y}}
to get:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}+|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2q_{\chi_{y}}(0,1,0)}\biggr]\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 213.39566pt\times(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}\biggl(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]\biggr)(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}T^{2}(\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{x}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}),

where in the last step we used Lemma
B.1
.

The other bounds follow similarly. We omit the details for brevity. To handle the last term, we see by boundedness of
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
from
A1)
and linear growth of
\chi_{y\bar{y}}
from
A7)
:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\bar{B}^{i,j,\epsilon,N}_{13}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\biggl[\tau_{1}^{2}(i)+\tau_{2}^{2}(i)\biggr]\chi_{y\bar{y}}(i,i)\psi(s,i)ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}CT\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\chi_{y\bar{y}}(i,i)|^{2}ds\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{\infty}\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a(N)}{N^{3/2}}CT^{2}(1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}\biggr])\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{\infty}\right\rVert}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a(N)}{N^{3/2}}CT^{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi_{\infty}\right\rVert}^{2}\textrm{ (by Lemma \ref{lemma:tildeYuniformbound}).}

∎

Proposition 6.4
.

Assume
A1)
-
A5)
. Let
F
be any function such that
\Xi
satisfies assumption
A8)
. Then for
\bar{F}(x,\mu)\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}}F(x,y,\mu)\pi(dy;x,\mu)
, with
\pi
as in Equation (
20
) and
\psi\in C^{1,2}_{b}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(F(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\bar{F}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dt\biggr|\biggr]\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 284.52756pt\leq C\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T+T^{1/2})\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}_{C_{b}^{1,2}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.1
, we can consider
\Xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
the unique classical solution to

(64)

\displaystyle L_{x,\mu}\Xi(x,y,\mu)

\displaystyle=-[F(x,y,\mu)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}F(x,y,\mu)\pi(dy;x,\mu)],\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Xi(x,y,\mu)\pi(dy;x,\mu)=0.

(
\Xi
and
F
may also depend on
t\in[0,T]
, but we suppress this in the notation here). Applying Itô’s formula to
\Xi^{N}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},(\tilde{X}^{1,\epsilon,N}_{t},...,\tilde{X}^{N,\epsilon,N}_{t}))\psi(t,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})
, where again
\Xi^{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the empirical projection of
\Xi
and using Proposition
D.6
, we get:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(F(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N})-\bar{F}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dt=\sum_{k=1}^{10}C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{k}(t)

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}[\Xi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{0})\psi(0,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})-\Xi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\psi(t,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})]

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\left(b(i)[\Xi_{x}(i)\psi(s,i)+\Xi(i)\psi_{x}(s,i)]+g(i)\Xi_{y}(i)\psi(s,i)+\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)[\Xi_{xy}(i)\psi(s,i)+\Xi_{y}(i)\psi_{x}(s,i)]\right)ds

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Xi(i)[j]\psi(s,i)ds

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon}{N}\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)\partial_{\mu}\Xi_{y}(i)[i]\psi(s,i)ds

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\Xi(i)\dot{\psi}(s,i)+c(i)[\Xi_{x}(i)\psi(s,i)+\Xi(i)\psi_{x}(s,i)]

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma^{2}(i)}{2}[\Xi_{xx}(i)\psi(s,i)+2\Xi_{x}(i)\psi_{x}(s,i)+\Xi(i)\psi_{xx}(s,i)+\frac{2}{N}\partial_{\mu}\Xi(i)[i]\psi_{x}(s,i)+\frac{2}{N}\partial_{\mu}\Xi_{x}(i)[i]\psi(s,i)]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\biggl\{c(j)\partial_{\mu}\Xi(i)[j]\psi(s,i)+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(j)[\frac{1}{N}\partial^{2}_{\mu}\Xi(i)[j,j]+\partial_{z}\partial_{\mu}\Xi(i)[j]]\psi(s,i)\biggr\}\biggr)ds

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{6}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Xi_{y}(i)\psi(s,i)dW^{i}_{s}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Xi_{y}(i)\psi(s,i)dB^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{7}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)[\Xi_{x}(i)\psi(s,i)+\Xi(i)\psi_{x}(s,i)]dW^{i}_{t}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(j)\partial_{\mu}\Xi(i)[j]\psi(s,i)dW^{j}_{s}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{8}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\sigma(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)[\Xi_{x}(i)\psi(s,i)+\Xi(i)\psi_{x}(s,i)]ds

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{9}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\biggl\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma(j)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{j}(s)\partial_{\mu}\Xi(i)[j]\psi(s,i)\biggr\}ds

\displaystyle C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{10}(t)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Xi_{y}(i)\psi(s,i)ds.

Then using that
\sigma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},
and
g
are bounded and that
b,c
grow at most linearly in
y
uniformly in
x,\mu
, and the assumptions on the growth of
\Xi
and its derivatives from
A8)
, the proof follows in essentially the same way as Propositions
6.1
and
6.3
.

Since
\Xi
is not necessarily bounded under Assumption
A8)
(
\tilde{q}_{\Xi}(n,l,\bm{\beta})\leq 1,\forall(n,l,\bm{\beta})\in\tilde{\bm{\zeta}}_{1}
allows
\Xi
to grow linearly in
y
), we need to handle the first term in the following way:

\displaystyle\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\Xi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{0})\psi(0,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})-\Xi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\psi(t,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr|\biggr]\leq

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}\biggl[1+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|\biggr]\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}_{\infty}\leq C(\rho)\epsilon^{2}a(N)\sqrt{N}\biggl[1+\epsilon^{-\rho}\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\psi\right\rVert}_{\infty}

for any
\rho\in(0,2)
by Lemma
B.2
. Taking any
\rho\in(0,1]
, the desired bound holds.

The only other terms that are handled differently in a way that matters are
C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)
, which corresponds to
\tilde{B}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)
, where the difference of having a
\epsilon
in front means that it is bounded by
\epsilon a(N)\leq\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}
, hence there being no need to include
a(N)/\sqrt{N}
in the definition of
C(N)
, and
C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t),C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t),C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{6}(t),
and
C^{i,\epsilon,N}_{10}(t)
, which were
O(1)
in Lemma
6.1
and hence were not shown to vanish.
C_{2}
is handled as
\tilde{B}_{3}
was,
C_{3}
in the same way that
\tilde{B}_{5}
was,
C_{6}
in the same way that
\tilde{B}_{6}
was, and
C_{10}
in the same way that
\tilde{B}_{7}
was.

∎

7.
Tightness of the Controlled Pair

In this section we throughout fix any controls satisfying the bound (
53
) and prove tightness of the pair
(\tilde{Z}^{N},Q^{N})
from Equations (
54
) and (
56
) under those controls. We will establish tightness in the appropriate spaces by proving tightness for each of the marginals.

As discussed in Section
2
, in order to prove tightness of the controlled fluctuation process
\tilde{Z}^{N}
in
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-m})
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large (see Equation (
5
)), we will use the theory of Mitoma from
[
36
]
. In particular, we need to prove uniform
m
-continuity for sufficiently large
m
in the family of Hilbert norms (
4
), and tightness of
\langle Z^{N},\phi\rangle
as a
C([0,T];\mathbb{R})
-valued random variables in order to apply Theorem 3.1 and Remark R1) in
[
36
]
. For the former, by definition we need some uniform in time control over the
\mathcal{S}_{-m}
-norm of
\tilde{Z}^{N}
. By Markov’s inequality, it suffices to show that
\sup_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{m}=1}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle|]\leq C
(see, e.g., the proof of
[
8
]
Theorem 4.7). As mentioned in Remark
5.1
, we will do so in Lemma
7.6
via triangle inequality and establishing an
L^{2}
rate of convergence of the controlled particle system (
55
) to the IID particle system (
57
), and a rate of convergence of the IID particle system (
57
) to the averaged McKean-Vlasov SDE (
25
). The convergence of the controlled particle system (
55
) to the IID particle system (
57
) is the subject of Subsection
7.2
and the convergence of the IID slow-fast McKean-Vlasov SDEs (
57
) to the averaged McKean-Vlasov SDE (
25
) is the subject of Subsection
7.1
.

A major difference between the coupling arguments in the references listed in Remark
5.1
and ours is that the IID system in the listed references were all equal in distribution to the law of the system which they are considering fluctuations from. This is not the case for us, since, as is well-known, we do not expect in general to have
L^{2}
convergence of fully-coupled slow-fast diffusions to their averaged limit (see
[
4
]
Remark 3.4.4 for an illustrative example). In other words, Lemma
7.5
cannot hold with
\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}
replaced by IID copies of the averaged limiting McKean-Vlasov Equation
X_{t}
from Equation (
25
). We are thus exploiting here the fact that the limits
\epsilon\downarrow 0
and
N\rightarrow\infty
commute, as shown in
[
7
]
, and hence we can use an IID system of Slow-Fast McKean-Vlasov SDEs as our intermediate process for our proof of tightness. This commutativity of the limits will hold so long as sufficient conditions for the propagation of chaos and stochastic averaging respectively hold for the system of SDEs (
55
) and the invariant measure
\pi
from Equation (
20
) is unique for all
x\in\mathbb{R},\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
. Recall that the latter is a consequence of assumptions
A1)
and
A2)
.

Tightness of
Q^{N}
is contained in Subsection
7.4
, and is essentially a consequence of moment bounds on the controlled particles (
55
), which again follow from the results of Section (
6
).

7.1.
On the Rate of Averaging for Fully-Coupled Slow-Fast McKean-Vlasov Diffusions

Here we recall a result which allows us to establish closeness of the slow-fast McKean-Vlasov SDEs (
57
) to the averaged McKean-Vlasov SDE (
25
). This result will be used in the Lemma
7.6
, which is a key ingredient in the proof of tightness of
\{\tilde{Z}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
. Therein, the first term being bounded is essentially due to the propagation of chaos holding for the controlled particle system (
55
), as captured by Lemma
7.5
. For the second term, the particles being IID means it is sufficient to gain control over convergence of
\sup_{\phi\in\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{m}=1}|\mathbb{E}[\phi(\bar{X}^{1,\epsilon}_{t})-\phi(X_{t})]|
as
\epsilon\downarrow 0
. There are very few results in the current literature in this direction. The existing averaging results for Slow-Fast McKean Vlasov SDEs can be found in
[
74
]
,
[
54
]
,
[
67
]
and in
[
5
]
. In
[
74
]
,
[
54
]
,
[
67
]
, only systems where
L^{2}
rates of averaging can be found are considered. Moreover, even for standard diffusion processes (which do not depend on their law), the only result for rates of convergence in distribution in the sense we desire for the fully-coupled setting is found in
[
26
]
Theorem 2.3. The fully coupled case for McKean-Vlasov diffusions is addressed in
[
5
]
.

We mention here the main result from
[
5
]
that will be used in our case. In particular, we wish to establish a rate of convergence in distribution of

(65)

\displaystyle\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=\eta^{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))+c(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr]ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))dW_{s}

\displaystyle\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=\eta^{y}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon}f(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))+g(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr]dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))dW_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))dB_{s}\biggr],

to the solution of Equation (
25
). Note that a solution to Equation (
65
) is equal in distribution to the IID particles from Equation (
57
).
The following moment bound holds.

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume
A1)
-
A2)
,
A4)
, and
A5)
. Then for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon>0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}_{t}|^{2p}\biggr]\leq C(p,T)+|\eta^{y}|^{2p}.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is omitted as it follows very closely the proof of Lemma 4.1 in
[
5
]
.
∎

Then, the main result of
[
5
]
that is relevant for our purposes is Theorem
7.2
.

Theorem 7.2

(Corollary 3.2 of
[
5
]
)
.

Assume that assumptions
A1)
-
A8)
as well as
A10)
-
A11)
hold. Then for
\phi\in C_{b,L}^{4}(\mathbb{R})
, there is a constant
C=C(T)
that is independent of
\phi
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\biggl|\mathbb{E}[\phi(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s})]-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{s})]\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C(T)|\phi|_{4},

where
\bar{X}^{\epsilon}
is as in Equation (
65
),
X
is as in Equation (
25
), and
|\cdot|_{4}
is as in Equation (
8
).

Remark 7.3
.

Though in
[
5
]
the assumptions are stated in terms of sufficient conditions on the limiting coefficients for the needed regularity of
\Phi,\chi,\Xi,\bar{\gamma},\bar{D}^{1/2},
and
\tilde{\chi}
in the proofs therein to hold (which is much easier to do in that situation since the lack of control eliminates the need for tracking specific rates of polynomial growth), it can be checked that the assumptions imposed on these functions by
A6)
,
A7)
,
A8)
,
A10)
, and
A11)
respectively are sufficient. See also Remark 2.6 therein.

In particular, in
[
5
]
, since specific rates of polynomial growth are not tracked, it is assumed the initial condition of
\bar{Y}^{\epsilon}
has all moments bounded. This holds automatically here, since
\eta^{y}\in\mathbb{R}
are deterministic. Then, due to Lemma
7.1
, it is sufficient to show the derivatives of the Poisson equations which show up in the proof have polynomial growth in
y
uniformly in
x,\mu,z
. In fact, the same Poisson equations are being used in Section 4 of
[
5
]
to gain ergodic-type theorems of the same nature as those of Section
6
here. The growth rates of
\Phi,\chi,\Xi
as imposed in
A6)
,
A7)
, and
A8)
are already required here for the ergodic-type theorems for the controlled system (
55
) found in Section
6
, and these conditions can be seen as more than sufficient for the results of
[
5
]
to go through. The solution
\tilde{\chi}
to Equation (
28
) does not, however, appear elsewhere in this paper, despite appearing in Proposition 4.4. of
[
5
]
, which is fundamental to the result presented here as Theorem
7.2
. This is why we can allow for the specified derivatives of
\tilde{\chi}
(which are exactly those appearing in the proof of Proposition 4.4.) in Assumption
A11)
to have polynomial growth of any order.

Lastly, we remark that the regularity of the limiting coefficients imposed by
A10)
is used not for ergodic-type theorems, but instead to establish regularity a Cauchy-Problem on Wasserstein space in Lemma 5.1 of
[
5
]
, which provides a refinement of Theorem 2.15 in
[
47
]
. As remarked therein, these assumptions can likely be relaxed via an alternative proof method, but as it stands these are the only results in this direction which provide sufficient regularity on the derivatives needed to prove Theorem
7.2
.

7.2.
Coupling of the Controlled Particles and the IID Slow-Fast McKean-Vlasov Particles

Here we establish a coupling result, which we will use along with Theorem
7.2
in order to establish tightness for the controlled fluctuation process
\{\tilde{Z}^{N}\}
from Equation (
54
). Recall the processes
(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})
that satisfy (
55
) and
(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})
that satisfies (
57
).

Lemma 7.4
.

Assume
A1)
-
A7)
,
A9)
, and
A12)
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl\{\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}

Proof.

We set
\tau_{1}\equiv 0
, since terms involving
\tau_{1}
can be handled in the same way as those involving
\tau_{2}
in the proof. By Itô’s formula, and given that the stochastic integrals are martingales (using Lemmas
B.1
and
7.1
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]=2\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\biggl(f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})
