\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\epsilon^{2}}|\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl(g(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-g(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon\sqrt{N}a(N)}\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(t)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\biggl(f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\biggl(f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\biggl(f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}|\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}|\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}|\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl(g(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-g(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon\sqrt{N}a(N)}\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(t)(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\biggl[-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\eta\epsilon^{2}}\biggl|f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2\epsilon^{2}}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\eta\epsilon^{2}}\biggl|f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2\epsilon^{2}}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}|\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}|\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\eta}\biggl|g(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-g(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2\epsilon^{2}}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\eta Na^{2}(N)}|\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})|^{2}|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta}{2\epsilon^{2}}|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})|^{2}\biggr]

for all
\eta>0
, where in the second inequality we used Equation (
17
) of Assumption
A2)
. Taking
\eta=\beta/8
and using the boundedness of
g
from Assumption
A5)
and of
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
from Assumption
A1)
, we get:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr|^{2}

\displaystyle+\biggl|f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr|^{2}

\displaystyle+|\tau_{2}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})|^{2}

\displaystyle+|\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-\tau_{2}(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{Na^{2}(N)}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\biggr]+C

Now using the global Lipschitz property of
f
from Assumption
A2)
and of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
from Assumption
A9)
to handle the terms of the form
|f(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})|^{2}
and Assumption
A12)
with Lemma
D.7
for the terms of the form

|f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})-f(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))|^{2},
we have:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}\biggr|^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2}N}+\frac{C}{Na^{2}(N)}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\biggr]+C.

When using Lipschitz continuity of
f,\tau_{1},
and
\tau_{2}
, we are also using that

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{2}(\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}\biggr|^{2}

by Equation (
80
) in Appendix
D
.

Now using a comparison theorem, dividing by
\frac{1}{N}
and summing from
i=1,...,N
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]\leq C\biggl\{e^{-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}s}ds+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}t}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]e^{\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}s}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N\epsilon^{2}}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}s}ds+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}e^{-\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}t}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\biggr]e^{\frac{\beta}{2\epsilon^{2}}s}ds\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl\{\epsilon^{2}+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\biggr]ds\biggr\}

and by the bound (
52
), we get:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl\{\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}+\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}.

∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Under assumptions
A1)
-
A9)
and
A12)
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}]

Proof.

Letting
(\bar{i})
denote the argument
(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))
,
(\tilde{i})
denote the argument
(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})
:

\displaystyle\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\left(b(\tilde{i})-b(\bar{i})\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left(c(\tilde{i})-c(\bar{i})\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(\tilde{i})\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sigma(\tilde{i})-\sigma(\bar{i})\right)dW^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\gamma(\tilde{i})-\gamma(\bar{i})\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left([\sigma(\tilde{i})+\tau_{1}(\tilde{i})\Phi_{y}(\tilde{i})]-[\sigma(\bar{i})+\tau_{1}(\bar{i})\Phi_{y}(\bar{i})]\right)dW^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\tau_{2}(\tilde{i})\Phi_{y}(\tilde{i})-\tau_{2}(\bar{i})\Phi_{y}(\bar{i})\right)dB^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle+R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)-R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)+R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)-R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)+R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t),

where here we recall
\Phi
from Equation (
22
) and
\gamma,\gamma_{1}
from Equation (
23
), and:

\displaystyle R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}b(\tilde{i})ds-\int_{0}^{t}\left(\gamma_{1}(\tilde{i})+[\frac{\tau_{1}(\tilde{i})}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(\tilde{i})}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(\tilde{i})\right)ds

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(\tilde{i})\Phi_{y}(\tilde{i})dW_{s}^{i}-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(\tilde{i})\Phi_{y}(\tilde{i})dB_{s}^{i}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\partial_{\mu}\Phi(\tilde{i})[\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s}]ds

\displaystyle R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}b(\bar{i})ds-\int_{0}^{t}\gamma_{1}(\bar{i})ds-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(\bar{i})\Phi_{y}(\bar{i})dW^{i}_{s}-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(\bar{i})\Phi_{y}(\bar{i})dB^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(x,y,\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\partial_{\mu}\Phi(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))[x]\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},Y^{\epsilon}_{s})(dx,dy)ds

\displaystyle R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\partial_{\mu}\Phi(\tilde{i})[\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s}]ds

\displaystyle R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(x,y,\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\partial_{\mu}\Phi(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))[x]\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s},Y^{\epsilon}_{s})(dx,dy)ds

\displaystyle R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sigma(\tilde{i})\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}+[\frac{\tau_{1}(\tilde{i})}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(\tilde{i})}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(\tilde{i})\right)ds

By Proposition
6.1
with
\psi\equiv 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{1}(t)|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}],

by Proposition 4.2 of
[
5
]
with
\psi\equiv 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{2}(t)|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\epsilon^{2},

by Proposition
6.3
with
\psi\equiv 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{3}(t)|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}],

and by Proposition 4.3 of
[
5
]
with
\psi\equiv 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{4}(t)|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\epsilon^{2}.

Here we are using that, under the assumed regularity of
\Phi
and
\chi
imposed by Assumptions
A6)
and
A7)
respectively along with the result of Lemma
7.1
, the norm can brought inside the expectation in Propositions 4.2 and 4.3 of
[
5
]
with little modification to the proofs.
Finally, since under Assumption
A6)

\Phi_{y}
is bounded:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|R^{i,\epsilon,N}_{5}(t)|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{a^{2}(N)N}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}ds\biggr]\leq C\frac{1}{a^{2}(N)N}

by the assumed bound on the controls (
52
). Now we see that, by Itô Isometry:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\gamma(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\gamma(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\right)ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|[\tau_{2}\Phi_{y}](\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-[\tau_{2}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]+R^{N}(t)

where
R^{N}(t)\leq C(T)[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}]
. We handle the terms from the martingales first.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N})\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N})-[\sigma+\tau_{1}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}|^{2}+|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}|^{2}ds\biggr]+\frac{C}{N}

by Lipschitz continuity from Assumption
A9)
and Assumption
A12)
with Lemma
D.7

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl\{\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}+\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{\tau\in[0,s]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{\tau}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{\tau}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]ds\biggr\}

\displaystyle\text{ by Lemma \ref{lemma:tildeYbarYdifference} }.

The exact same proof and bound applies to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|[\tau_{2}\Phi_{y}](\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-[\tau_{2}\Phi_{y}](\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr].

In a similar manner:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\gamma(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\gamma(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\right)ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(T)}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|\gamma(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\gamma(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N})\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|\gamma(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N})-\gamma(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{s}))\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(T)}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}|^{2}+|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}-\bar{Y}^{i,\epsilon}_{s}|^{2}ds\biggr]+\frac{C(T)}{N}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\biggl\{\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}+\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{\tau\in[0,s]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{\tau}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{\tau}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]ds\biggr\}.

Collecting these bounds, we have for all
p\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{p}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{p}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(T)\int_{0}^{p}\sup_{\tau\in[0,s]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{\tau}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{\tau}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]ds+C_{2}(T)[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}]

so

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in[0,t]}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{p}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{p}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\left[\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{\tau\in[0,s]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{\tau}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{\tau}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]ds+[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}]\right]

and by Gronwall’s inequality:

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{p}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{p}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)[\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{Na^{2}(N)}].

∎

7.3.
Tightness of
\tilde{Z}^{N}

We now have the tools to prove tightness of
\{\tilde{Z}^{N}\}
from Equation (
54
). We first prove a uniform-in-time bound on
\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle
in terms of
|\cdot|_{4}
(recall Equation (
8
)) in Lemma
7.6
. Then, using the results from Section
6
, we provide a prelimit representation for
\tilde{Z}^{N}
which is a priori
\mathcal{O}(1)
in
\epsilon,N
in Lemma
7.7
. Combining these two lemmas, we are then able to establish tightness via the methods of
[
36
]
in Proposition
7.8
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Under Assumptions
A1)
-
A12)
, there exists
C
independent of
N
such that for all
\phi\in C_{b,L}^{4}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|\phi|^{2}_{4}.

Proof.

Let
\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t}
be as in Equation (
58
). Then, by triangle inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2a^{2}(N)N\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\langle\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle-\langle\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle|^{2}\biggr]+2a^{2}(N)N\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\langle\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle-\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{t}),\phi\rangle|^{2}\biggr].

For the first term:

\displaystyle a^{2}(N)N\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\langle\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle-\langle\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=a^{2}(N)N\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})-\phi(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}(N)N\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\phi(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t})-\phi(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}(N)N\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)[\epsilon^{2}a^{2}(N)N+a^{2}(N)+1]\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi^{\prime}\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty},

where in the second to last inequality we used Lemma
7.5
.

For the second term, we use that
\{\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
are IID to see:

\displaystyle a^{2}(N)N\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\langle\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle-\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{t}),\phi\rangle|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=a^{2}(N)N\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{t})]\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=a^{2}(N)\biggl\{(N-1)(\mathbb{E}[\phi(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{t})])^{2}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\phi(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{t})]\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}(N)\biggl\{N(\mathbb{E}[\phi(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t})]-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{t})])^{2}+4\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{\infty}^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq a^{2}(N)N\epsilon^{2}C(T)|\phi|^{2}_{4}+4a^{2}(N)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{\infty},

where in the last inequality we used Theorem
7.2
. This bound vanishes as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Assume
A1)
-
A8)
,
A10)
, and
A13)
.
Define
\bar{L}_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}
to be the operator parameterized by
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
which acts on
\phi\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
by

(66)

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=\bar{\gamma}(x,\nu_{2})\phi^{\prime}(x)+\bar{D}(x,\nu_{2})\phi^{\prime\prime}(x)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(z,(1-r)\nu_{1}+r\nu_{2})[x]\phi^{\prime}(z)+\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{D}(z,(1-r)\nu_{1}+r\nu_{2})[x]\phi^{\prime\prime}(z)dr\nu_{1}(dz).

Here we recall
\bar{\gamma},\bar{D}
from Equation (
24
), the linear functional derivative from Definition
D.4
,
\Phi
from Equation (
22
), and the occupation measures
Q^{N}
from Equation (
56
).
Then we have the representation: for
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R^{N}_{t}(\phi)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|R^{N}_{t}(\phi)\biggr|\biggr]\leq\bar{R}(N,T)|\phi|_{4},

\bar{R}(N,T)\rightarrow 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and
\bar{R}(N,T)
is independent of
\phi
.

Proof.

Recall
\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}
from Equation (
54
),
X_{t}
from Equation (
25
), and that
\tilde{Z}^{N}=a(N)\sqrt{N}[\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}-\mathcal{L}(X)].

By Itô’s formula,

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\phi(x)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+c(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(i)\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}

where
(i)
denotes the argument
(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})
and

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\phi(x)+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})+\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{2}\bar{D}^{1/2}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s})\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})dW_{s}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\phi(x)+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})+\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\right)ds\biggr]

since
\bar{D}^{1/2}
is bounded as per Assumption
A10)
. Then

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle=\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+c(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(i)\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\right)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})+\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\right)ds\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\gamma(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left(D(i)\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds

\displaystyle+R^{i}_{1}(t)+R^{i}_{2}(t)+M^{i}(t)\biggr\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\left([\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)\right)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

where here we recall
\gamma_{1},\gamma,D,D_{1}
from Equation (
23
) and:

\displaystyle R^{i}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{0}^{t}\biggl(\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\int_{0}^{t}\gamma_{1}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+D_{1}(i)\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})]

\displaystyle+[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)ds

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}-\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dB^{i}_{s}-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds

\displaystyle R^{i}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds

\displaystyle M^{i}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{0}^{t}[\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)+\sigma(i)]\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dB^{i}_{s}.

For
R^{i}_{1}(t)
, we have via Proposition
6.1
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|R^{i}_{1}(t)|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C[\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T+T^{1/2})+a(N)T]|\phi|_{3}.

For
R^{i}_{2}(t)
, we have via Proposition
6.3
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|R^{i}_{2}(t)|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C[\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T+T^{1/2})+\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}T]|\phi|_{3}.

For
M^{i}(t)
, we have by Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}M^{i}(t)\biggr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\biggl\{\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}[\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)+\sigma(i)]^{2}|\phi^{\prime}(i)|^{2}ds\biggr)^{1/2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}|\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)|^{2}|\phi^{\prime}(i)|^{2}ds\biggr)^{1/2}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq Ca(N)T^{1/2}|\phi|_{1}

since the integrand it bounded by Assumptions
A1)
,
A5)
, and
A6)
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle=

\displaystyle=\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\gamma(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left(D(i)\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R_{t}^{3,N}(\phi)

where
\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|R_{t}^{3,N}(\phi)|\biggr]\leq C(T)\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}\vee a(N)|\phi|_{3}
. We rewrite this as:

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{\gamma}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{D}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds+R_{4}^{i}(t)+R_{5}^{i}(t)\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R_{t}^{3,N}(\phi)

where

\displaystyle R_{4}^{i}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}[\gamma(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\bar{\gamma}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}_{s})]\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds

\displaystyle R_{5}^{i}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}[D(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\bar{D}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})]\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})ds.

By Proposition
6.4
(using here Assumption
A8)
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|R^{i}_{4}(t)|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T^{1/2}+T)|\phi|_{3}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|R^{i}_{5}(t)|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}(1+T^{1/2}+T)|\phi|_{4}.

Now, we arrive at

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{a(N)}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{\gamma}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{\gamma}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\bar{D}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{D}(X_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(X_{s})\biggr]\right)ds\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R_{t}^{N}(\phi)

where
\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|R_{t}^{N}(\phi)|\biggr]\leq C(T)[\epsilon a(N)\sqrt{N}\vee a(N)]|\phi|_{4}
. By Assumption
A13)
,
\bar{\gamma}
and
\bar{D}
have well-defined linear functional derivatives (see Definition
D.4
). Then we can rewrite
\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle
as

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)+\bar{D}(\cdot,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\rangle ds

\displaystyle+a(N)\sqrt{N}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),[\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)+[\bar{D}(\cdot,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})-\bar{D}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))]\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\rangle ds\biggr]

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R^{N}_{t}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)+\bar{D}(\cdot,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\rangle ds

\displaystyle+a(N)\sqrt{N}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\biggl[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,(1-r)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})+r\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})[y](\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}_{s}(dy)-\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dy))dr\biggr]\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)

\displaystyle+\biggl[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{D}(\cdot,(1-r)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})+r\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})[y](\tilde{\mu}^{N,\epsilon}_{s}(dy)-\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dy))dr\biggr]\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\rangle ds\biggr]

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R^{N}_{t}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)+R^{N}_{t}(\phi).

∎

Proposition 7.8
.

Under Assumptions
A1)
-
A13)
,
\{\tilde{Z}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight as a sequence of
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-m})
-valued random variables, where
m
is as in Equation (
6
).

Proof.

By Remark R.1 on p.997 of
[
36
]
, it suffices to prove tightness of
\{\langle\tilde{Z}^{N},\phi\rangle\}
as a sequence of
C([0,T];\mathbb{R})
-valued random variables for each
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
, along with uniform
7
-continuity as defined in the same remark. By the argument found in the proof of
[
8
]
Theorem 4.7, to show the latter it suffices to prove:

(67)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle\biggr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{7},\forall\phi\in\mathcal{S}.

After these two results are shown, we will have established tightness of
\tilde{Z}^{N}
as
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-w})
-valued random variables for
m>7
such that the canonical embedding
\mathcal{S}_{-7}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{-m}
is Hilbert-Schmidt. We start with showing tightness of
\{\langle\tilde{Z}^{N},\phi\rangle\}
. By Lemma
7.7
, we write for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=A^{N}_{t}(\phi)+R^{N}_{t}(\phi)

\displaystyle A^{N}_{t}(\phi)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

where for each
\phi
,
R^{N}(\phi)\rightarrow 0
in
C([0,T];\mathbb{R})
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, to prove tightness of
\{\langle\tilde{Z}^{N},\phi\rangle\}
, it is sufficient to prove tightness of
\{A^{N}(\phi)\}
.
We note that for any and
0\leq\tau<t\leq T
:

\displaystyle A^{N}_{t}(\phi)-A^{N}_{\tau}(\phi)

\displaystyle=B^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)+C^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)+D^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)

\displaystyle B^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\tau}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds

\displaystyle C^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[\tau,t]}\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle D^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[\tau,t]}[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds).

Then we have for
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{|t-\tau|\leq\delta}\biggl|B^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)\biggr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|^{2}ds\biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{1/2}T^{1/2}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{1/2}T^{1/2}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\sup_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\delta^{1/2}|\phi|_{6}

by boundedness of the first 5 derivatives in
x
of
\bar{\gamma},\bar{D}
, and of the first 5 derivatives in
z
of
\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma},\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{D}
from Assumption
A13)
, the definition of
\bar{L}_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}
from Equation (
66
), and Lemma
7.6
. Also, we see:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{|t-\tau|\leq\delta}\biggl|D^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)\biggr|\biggr]\leq

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{|t-\tau|\leq\delta}\int_{\tau}^{t}\left([|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|]|\Phi_{y}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})|\right)ds\biggr]|\phi|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}ds\biggr]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{|t-\tau|\leq\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{\tau}^{t}|\Phi_{y}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})|^{2}ds\biggr]^{1/2}|\phi|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{|t-\tau|\leq\delta}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{\tau}^{t}|\Phi_{y}(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})|^{2}ds\biggr]^{1/2}|\phi|_{1}\text{ by the bound \eqref{eq:controlassumptions0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{1/2}C(T)|\phi|_{1}\text{ by the boundedness of $\Phi_{y}$ from Assumption \ref{assumption:multipliedpolynomialgrowth}.}

The proof that
\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|B^{N}_{t,\tau}(\phi)\biggr|\biggr]\leq C\delta^{1/2}C(T)|\phi|_{1}
holds in the same way.

So by the Arzelà-Ascoli tightness criterion on classical Wiener space (see, e.g. Theorem 4.10 in Chapter 2 of
[
23
]
), we have
\{A^{N}(\phi)\}
and hence
\{\langle\tilde{Z}^{N},\phi\rangle\}
are tight as a sequence of
C([0,T];\mathbb{R})
-valued random variables for each
\phi
.

Now we see by the same argument (fixing
\tau=0
) and the fact that, as shown in Lemma
7.7
,

\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|R^{N}_{t}(\phi)\biggr|\biggr]\leq\bar{R}(N,T)|\phi|_{4}
with
\bar{R}(N,T)\rightarrow 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
:

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle\biggr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)|\phi|_{6}\leq C(T)\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{7}

for all
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
, where here we used the inequality (
9
). Thus the bound (
67
) holds, and tightness is established.
∎

7.4.
Tightness of
Q^{N}

The proof of tightness of
\{Q^{N}\}
from Equation (
56
) is standard, see
[
43
,
3
,
7
]
. We see that since the occupation measures
Q^{N}
involve
\{\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
from Equation (
55
) as part of their definition, we will need some kind of uniform control on their expectation. Thus, we begin with a lemma:

Lemma 7.9
.

Under assumptions
A1)
-
A7)
and
A9)
, we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]<\infty.

Proof.

Using that

\displaystyle\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}

\displaystyle=\eta^{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)+c(i)\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)dW^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle=\eta^{x}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)ds-\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\gamma_{1}(i)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)dW^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)dB^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)ds\biggr\}+\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(i)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(i)dW^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)dW^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)dB^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}+[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)\right)ds,

where here once again the argument
(i)
is denoting
(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})
and similarly for
j
, and we recall
\Phi
from Equation (
22
) and
\gamma_{1},\gamma
from Equation (
23
).

So, by Itô Isometry and boundedness of
\sigma
from
A5)
, of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
from
A1)
, and of
\Phi_{y}
from
A6)
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(|\eta^{x}|^{2}+T)+\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{R^{i,N}_{1}(t)+R^{i,N}_{2}(t)+R^{i,N}_{3}(t)+R^{i,N}_{4}(t)\biggr\}

\displaystyle R^{i,N}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)ds-\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\gamma_{1}(i)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{1}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)dW^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\tau_{2}(i)\Phi_{y}(i)dB^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]ds+\int_{0}^{t}[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)ds\biggr\}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle R^{i,N}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\gamma(i)ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle R^{i,N}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}b(j)\partial_{\mu}\Phi(i)[j]ds\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle R^{i,N}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}+[\frac{\tau_{1}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)+\frac{\tau_{2}(i)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)]\Phi_{y}(i)ds\right)\biggr|^{2}\biggr]

Then applying Proposition
6.1
with
\psi=1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}R^{i,N}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C[\epsilon^{2}(1+T+T^{2})+\frac{1}{N}T^{2}]

Using Assumption
A9)
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}R^{i,N}_{2}(t)\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}T\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}|\gamma(i)|^{2}ds\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq CT\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}+|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|\tilde{X}^{j,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}\biggr]ds

\displaystyle\leq CT^{2}+CT\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}\biggr]ds

by Lemma
B.1
.
Applying Proposition
6.3
with
\psi=1
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}R^{i,N}_{3}(t)\leq C[\epsilon^{2}(1+T+T^{2})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}T^{2}]

Using the boundedness of
\sigma
from
A5)
, of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
from
A1)
, and of
\Phi_{y}
from
A6)
and the bound (
53
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}R^{i,N}_{4}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{CT}{a^{2}(N)N}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\left(|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\right)ds]\leq\frac{CT}{a^{2}(N)N}.

Then, by Gronwall’s inequality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq C(T)[1+\epsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\frac{1}{a^{2}(N)N}]\leq C(T),

since all the above terms which depend on
N,\epsilon
in the first bound vanish as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
Since this holds uniformly in
N
and
t
, we are done.
∎

Now we can prove tightness of the occupation measures.

Proposition 7.10
.

Under assumptions
A1)
-
A7)
and
A9)
,
\{Q^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight as a sequence of
M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
-valued random variables (recall this space of measures introduced above Equation (
10
)).

Proof.

Consider the function
G\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given by

\displaystyle G(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z|^{2}+|y|^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)\theta(dx,dy,dz,ds).

Then we have
G
is bounded below, and considering a given level set

A_{L}\coloneqq\{\theta\in M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}G(\theta)\leq L\},

it follows by Chebyshev’s inequality that
\sup_{\theta\in A_{L}}\theta((K^{\epsilon}_{L})^{c})\leq\epsilon

where
K^{\epsilon}_{L}
is the compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times[0,T]

\displaystyle K^{\epsilon}_{L}\coloneqq\{(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}+|z|^{2}\leq\frac{L}{\epsilon}\}\times[0,T].

We also see that any collection of measures on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times[0,T]
which is in
M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
is uniformly bounded in the total variation norm, and that for
\{\theta^{N}\}\subset A_{L}
such that
\theta^{N}\rightarrow\theta
in
M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
(recalling here that we are using the topology of weak convergence), by a version of Fatou’s lemma (see Theorem A.3.12 in
[
13
]
)

\displaystyle G(\theta)\leq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}G(\theta^{N})\leq L,

so
\theta\in A_{L}
. Via Prokhorov’s theorem,
A_{L}
is precompact, and we have shown that
A_{L}
is closed, and hence
G
has compact level sets. Thus
G
is a tightness function (see
[
13
]
p.309), and it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[G(Q^{N})\biggr]=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\left(|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}+|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\right)ds\biggr]<\infty

to see that
\{Q^{N}\}
is a tight sequence of
\mathcal{M}_{R}(\mathbb{R}^{4})-
valued random variables. This follows immediately from the bound (
52
) and Lemmas
B.1
and
7.9
.
∎

8.
Identification of the Limit

Now having established tightness of
\{(\tilde{Z}^{N},Q^{N})\}_{\mathbb{N}}
, we take any sub-sequence that converges in distribution as
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-m})\times M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
-valued random variables, and call the random variable which is its limit
(Z,Q)
. We will show that
Q\in P^{*}(Z)
, and that this uniquely characterizes the distribution of
(Z,Q)
for a given choice of controls in the construction of
Q^{N}.
We will at times apply Skorokhod’s Representation Theorem to without loss of generality pose the problem on a probability space such that this subsequence converges to
(Z,Q)
almost surely. We also do not distinguish from the subsequence and the original sequence in the notation, nor the original probability space and that invoked by Skorokhod’s Representation Theorem.
We begin with two lemmas which allow us to identify convergence of the controlled empirical measure
\tilde{\mu}^{N}
from (
54
) to the law of the averaged McKean-Vlasov equation (
25
):

Lemma 8.1
.

In the setting of Proposition
7.9
, we have for any
p\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\epsilon>0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}|^{p}\biggr]\leq|\eta^{x}|^{p}+C(T,p)[1+|\eta^{y}|^{p}].

Here
\bar{X}^{\epsilon}
is as in Equation (
65
). That is, it is equal in distribution to the IID particles from Equation (
57
).

Proof.

This follows in the same way as Lemmas
7.5
and
7.9
, using Lemma
7.1
and the ergodic-type Theorems from Section 4 of
[
5
]
. We omit the proof for brevity.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

Assume
A1)
-
A12)
. Let
\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t}
be as in Equation (
54
), with controls satisfying (
53
). Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{W}_{2}(\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))\biggr]\rightarrow 0\text{ as }N\rightarrow\infty,\forall t\in[0,T],

where
X_{t}
is as in Equation (
25
). In particular, decomposing
Q^{N}
from Equation (
56
) as
Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,dt)=Q^{N}_{t}(dx,dy,dz)dt
, for any
t\in[0,T]
, the first marginal of
Q^{N}_{t}
converges to
\mathcal{L}(X_{t})
in probability as a sequence of
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
-valued random variables.

Proof.

Firstly, we note by Theorem
7.2
,
\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(X_{t})
in
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
(using here that
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
is convergence determining - see
[
19
]
Proposition 3,4.4). In addition, by Lemma
8.1
, we have
\sup_{\epsilon>0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|x|^{p}\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t})(dx)<\infty
, for some
p>2
. Thus, we have by uniform integrability,
\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}|^{2}\biggr]\rightarrow\mathbb{E}\biggl[|X_{t}|^{2}\biggr]
as
\epsilon\downarrow 0
, so
\mathbb{W}_{2}(\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}),\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))\rightarrow 0
as
\epsilon\downarrow 0
(Theorem 5.5 in
[
9
]
).
By Lemma
7.5
, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{W}_{2}(\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl|\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{t}-\bar{X}^{i,\epsilon}_{t}\biggr|^{2}\biggr]\rightarrow 0\text{ as }N\rightarrow\infty,

where
\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}
is as in Equation (
58
).
Also, by Glivenko-Cantelli Convergence in the Wasserstein Distance (see, e.g. Section 5.1.2 in
[
9
]
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{W}_{2}(\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr]\rightarrow 0\text{ as }N\rightarrow\infty.

So by the triangle inequality (see, e.g. the proof of
[
9
]
Proposition 5.3), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{W}_{2}(\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{W}_{2}(\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t})\biggr]+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbb{W}_{2}(\bar{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t},\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}))\biggr]+\mathbb{W}_{2}(\mathcal{L}(\bar{X}^{\epsilon}_{t}),\mathcal{L}(X_{t}))\rightarrow 0\text{ as }N\rightarrow\infty.

The latter statement of the Lemma now follows from the construction of
Q^{N}
and Markov’s inequality.
∎

Now we can use the prelimit representation for the controlled fluctuation process
\tilde{Z}^{N}
from Lemma
7.7
in order to identify the limiting behavior of
(\tilde{Z}^{N},Q^{N})
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Under assumptions
A1)
-
A13)
,
(Z,Q)
satisfies Equation (
32
) with probability 1.

Proof.

We now invoke the Skorokhod’s Representation Theorem as previously discussed. By a standard density argument, we can simply show that Equation (
32
) holds with probability 1 for each
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
t\in[0,T]
. This is using the fact that there exists a countable, dense collection of smooth, compactly supported functions in
\mathcal{S}_{m}
(this follows from, e.g. Corollary 2.1.2 in
[
62
]
).

We note that by almost sure convergence of
\tilde{Z}^{N}
to
Z
, we have for each
t\in[0,T]
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{t},\phi\rangle\rightarrow\langle Z_{t},\phi\rangle
with probability 1. We also note that the prelimit representation given in Lemma
7.7
can be written solely in terms of
Q^{N}
and
Z^{N}
by replacing
\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}
by the first marginal of
Q^{N}_{s}
. We can therefore take
\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}
to also live on the new probability space from Skorokhod’s Representation Theorem, and on that space we still have the convergence of
\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{t}
to
\mathcal{L}(X_{t})
in probability proved in Lemma
8.2
. Thus, by the representation provided by Lemma
7.7
, we only need to show the limits in probability:

(68)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds\rightarrow(N\rightarrow\infty)\int_{0}^{t}\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds

(69)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\left(\sigma(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)+[\tau_{1}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi^{\prime}(x)\right)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)

\displaystyle\rightarrow(N\rightarrow\infty)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,t]}\left(\sigma(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))z_{1}\phi^{\prime}(x)+[\tau_{1}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))z_{1}+\tau_{2}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))z_{2}]\Phi_{y}(x,y,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(x)\right)Q(dx,dy,dz,ds),

where
\bar{L}_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}
is as in Equation (
66
) and
\bar{L}_{\nu}
is as in Equation (
32
).
By boundedness and continuity of
\bar{\gamma},\bar{D}
from assumption
A13)
(see Definition
D.4
), along with Lemma
8.2
, we have for each
s\in[0,T]
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, the limit in probability

\displaystyle\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\text{ in }\mathcal{S}_{m}

holds via the continuous mapping theorem. Thus, for each
s\in[0,T]
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, the limit in probability

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\rightarrow\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle

holds. We have, then, for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\right)ds\biggr|\biggr]\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\leq\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|ds\biggr],

and we have by Lemma
7.6
that

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)-\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|^{2}\biggr]ds<\infty,

so by uniform integrability we can pass to the limit to get

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|ds\biggr]=

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s}),\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|ds\biggr]=0.

Similarly, we can use that by the Lemma
7.6
and Fatou’s lemma:

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|^{2}\biggr]ds<\infty

and to get

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\right)ds\biggr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\biggl|\langle\tilde{Z}^{N}_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle-\langle Z_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle\biggr|ds\biggr]

\displaystyle=0.

Then, by Markov’s inequality, we establish (
68
). The limit (
69
) follows immediately from the integrand being bounded by
C[|z_{1}|+|z_{2}|]
and continuous in
\mathbb{W}_{2}
, along with the assumed bound on the controls (
53
) (see, e.g.,
[
13
]
Theorem A.3.18).
∎

Proposition 8.4
.

Under assumptions
A1)
-
A13)
,
Q\in P^{*}(Z)
with Probability 1.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.3
,
(

P
enumi

1)
in the definition of
P^{*}(Z)
holds. It remains to show
(

P
enumi

2)
-
(

P
enumi

4)
.

(

P
enumi

4)
is immediate from the fact that the last marginal of
Q
is Lebesgue measure by the definition of
M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
above Equation (
10
), and the first marginal of
\tilde{Q}^{N}_{s}
is
\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}
, which converges in
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and hence
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
to
\mathcal{L}(X_{s})
by Lemma
8.2
.

(

P
enumi

2)
follows from the version of Fatou’s lemma from Theorem A.3.12 in
[
13
]
, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,dt)
is a non-negative random variable, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q(dx,dy,dz,dt)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,dt)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\biggl]ds<\infty

by the assumed bound (
52
).

Lastly, to see
(

P
enumi

3)
, take
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(U\times\mathbb{R})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
. Here
U
is an open interval in
\mathbb{R}
containing
[0,T]
. Then
applying Itô’s formula (recalling here
\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}
from Equation (
55
)):

\displaystyle\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{T})\psi(T,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{T})

\displaystyle=\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})\psi(0,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})+\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\dot{\psi}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\biggl[f(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}(\tau_{1}^{2}(i)+\tau_{2}^{2}(i))\phi^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr]\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl[g(i)+\tau_{1}(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}+\tau_{2}(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{2}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\biggr]\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+\biggl[c(i)+\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\biggr]\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(i)\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{xx}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}b(i)\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\tau_{1}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\tau_{2}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dB^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\sigma(i)\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}

where
(i)
denotes the argument
(\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s},\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s})
. So recalling the definition of
L_{x,\mu}
from Equation (
18
), multiplying both sides by
\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{N}
and summing,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}L_{x,\tilde{\mu}^{\epsilon,N}_{s}}\phi(y)\psi(s,x)Q^{N}(dx,dy,dz,ds)=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl\{\epsilon^{2}[\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})\psi(0,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{0})-\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{T})\psi(T,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{T})]

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\dot{\psi}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\biggl[c(i)+\sigma(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\biggr]\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}(i)\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{xx}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)ds

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\biggl[g(i)+\tau_{1}(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}+\tau_{2}(i)\frac{\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{2}(s)}{a(N)\sqrt{N}}\biggr]\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})

\displaystyle+b(i)\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})+\sigma(i)\tau_{1}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\biggr)ds

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\tau_{1}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}+\epsilon\int_{0}^{T}\tau_{2}(i)\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dB^{i}_{s}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\sigma(i)\phi(\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})\psi_{x}(s,\tilde{X}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s})dW^{i}_{s}.

Since all terms in the right hand side are bounded other than
b
and
c
, which grow at most linearly in
y
as per Assumption
A5)
, we see after using the bound (
52
) that the right hand side is bounded in square expectation by

\displaystyle C(T)\epsilon^{2}(1+\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\biggl[|\tilde{Y}^{i,\epsilon,N}_{s}|^{2}\biggr])\leq C(T)\epsilon^{2}

by Lemma
B.1
, and hence converges to
0
in probability.

We can see also by the fact that
\phi
and
\psi
are compactly supported and the coefficients in
L_{x,\mu}
are continuous in
(x,y,\mathbb{W}_{2})
by assumptions
A1)
and
A2)
, we can use the definition of convergence in
M_{T}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
and Lemma
8.2
to see the left hand side converges in probability to

\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(y)\psi(s,x)Q(dx,dy,dz,ds)

(see, e.g.,
[
13
]
Theorem A.3.18). Thus, using that
Q
satisfies
(

P
enumi

4)
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(y)\psi(s,x)Q(dx,dy,dz,ds)=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(y)\psi(s,x)\lambda(dy;x,s)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dx)ds=0

for some stochastic kernel
\lambda
almost surely.
Then noting that by boundedness of the coefficients and the derivatives of
\phi
, we have
(s,x)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}}L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(y)\lambda(dy;x,s)
is in
L^{1}_{\text{loc}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})})
for all
\phi
, and thus by Corollary 22.38 (2) in
[
16
]
, for each
\phi
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(y)\lambda(dy;x,s)=0

\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})}
- almost surely. By a standard density argument (see
[
7
]
Section 6.2.1), we have by letting

\displaystyle A=\{(s,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(y)\lambda(dy;x,s)=0,\forall\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\},

\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})}(A\times[0,T])=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dx)ds=1
. This then characterizes
\lambda(dy;x,s)
as
\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})}-
almost surely satisfying
L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}^{*}\lambda(\cdot;x,s)=0
in the distributional sense, and by definition of stochastic kernels
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\lambda(dy;x,s)=1,\forall x,s
, so
\lambda(dy;x,s)
is an invariant measure associated to
L_{x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}
. Since such an invariant measure is unique under assumptions
A1)
and
A2)
by
[
72
]
Proposition 1, we have in fact
\lambda(dy;x,s)=\pi(dy;x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))

\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})}-
almost surely.

∎

8.1.
Weak-Sense Uniqueness

In order to prove the Laplace Principle Lower bound (
31
) in Section
10
and compactness of level sets in Proposition
10.2
, we will need to be able to identify a given
Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-w/r})
using only the information that
Z
solves the limiting controlled Equation (
32
) for some fixed
Q
. Hence, in this subsection, we prove an appropriate notion of weak-sense uniqueness for Equation (
32
). Recall the space spaces
\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}_{-p}
, and the related norms from the beginning of Section
2
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and consider
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{p+2}
,
F\in C_{b}^{p}(\mathbb{R})
, and
G\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
. Then for any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

(1)

\langle\phi,F\phi^{\prime}\rangle_{p}\leq C\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}

(2)

\langle\phi,F\phi^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{p}\leq C\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+x^{2})^{p}|\phi^{(p+1)}(x)|^{2}F(x)dx

(3)

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{R}}G(\cdot)\phi^{(k)}(z)\mu(dz)\right\rVert}_{p}\leq C\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{k+1}
, for
k\leq p-1
.

Proof.

The proof of (1) follows by the same integration by parts argument as A1) in the Appendix of
[
39
]
. Part 2 follows by the same integration by parts argument as A2) in the Appendix of
[
39
]
. It becomes evident upon reading those proofs that
w_{p}\coloneqq(1+x^{2})^{p}
can be replaced by any
w_{p}
such that
w^{-1}_{p}D^{k}w_{p}
is bounded for all
k\leq p
. The proof of 3 is similar to the proof of A4) in the Appendix of
[
39
]
. We recall it here:

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{R}}G(\cdot)\phi^{(k)}(z)\mu(dz)\right\rVert}_{p}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\sum_{j=0}^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+x^{2})^{2p}\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}}G^{(j)}(x)\phi^{(k)}(z)\mu(dz)\biggr|^{2}dx\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert G\right\rVert}_{p}\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\phi^{(k)}(z)|^{2}\mu(dz)\biggr)^{1/2}\text{ by H\"{o}lder's inequality}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert G\right\rVert}_{p}|\phi|_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert G\right\rVert}_{p}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{k+1}.

∎

Lemma 8.6
.

Under assumption
A13)
, for any
p\in\{1,...,w+2\}
, where
w
is as in Equation (
6
), and any
s\in[0,T]
,
\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}
as given in Equation (
32
), where
X_{s}
is as in Equation (
25
), is a bounded linear map from
\mathcal{S}_{p+2}
to
\mathcal{S}_{p}
. In particular, there exists
c_{p}
such that for all
s\in[0,T]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{p+2}
,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi\right\rVert}_{p}\leq c_{p}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p+2}.

The same holds with
w
replaced by
r
from Equation (
7
) if we in addition assume
A’13)
.

Proof.

Linearity is clear. For
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{p+2}
and
s\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+x^{2})^{2p}\biggl([\bar{\gamma}(x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(x)]^{(k)}\biggr)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\sum_{k=0}^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+x^{2})^{2p}\biggl(\phi^{(k+1)}(x)\biggr)^{2}dx\text{ by Assumption \ref{assumption:limitingcoefficientsregularity}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{p+1}.

In the same way, we can see

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\bar{D}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}\leq c_{p}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{p+2}.

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\phi^{\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}=\sum_{k=0}^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+x^{2})^{2p}\biggl(\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial x^{k}}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[x]\phi^{\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\biggr]\biggr)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)|\phi|^{2}_{1}\text{ by Jensen's inequality and Tonelli's Theorem}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c_{p}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}\text{ by Assumption \ref{assumption:limitingcoefficientsregularity} and the inequality \eqref{eq:sobolembedding}}.

Again, in the same way, we can see

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{D}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\phi^{\prime\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\right\rVert}^{2}_{p}

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{3}^{2},

so by definition of
\bar{L}_{\nu}
, the result follows.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Under Assumption
A13)
, we have for any
p\in\{1,...,w\}
and
F\in\mathcal{S}_{-p}
, where
w
is as in Equation (
6
),

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\langle F,\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}F\rangle_{-(p+2)}\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}^{2}_{-(p+2)}

where
\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{S}_{-p}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{-(p+2)}
is the adjoint of
\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{S}_{p+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{p}
given in Equation (
32
) (using here Lemma
8.6
). The same holds if instead we further assume
A’13)
and replace
w
with
r
from Equation (
7
).

Proof.

By the Riesz representation theorem we can take
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
such that for all
\psi\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
,
\langle F,\psi\rangle=\langle\phi,\psi\rangle_{p}
and
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}_{-p}=\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p}
. By a density argument, we may assume in fact that
\phi\in\mathcal{S},

\langle F,\psi\rangle=\langle\phi,\psi\rangle_{p+2}
, and
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p+2}=\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}_{-(p+2)}
. Then for any
s\in[0,T]
,
\langle F,\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}F\rangle_{-(p+2)}=\langle F,\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi\rangle=\langle\phi,\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi\rangle_{p+2}.
Then,

\displaystyle\langle\phi,\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi\rangle_{p+2}=

\displaystyle=\langle\phi,\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)\rangle_{p+2}+\langle\phi,\bar{D}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\rangle_{p+2}+\langle\phi,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\phi^{\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\rangle_{p+2}

\displaystyle+\langle\phi,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{D}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\phi^{\prime\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\rangle_{p+2}

\displaystyle\leq\langle\phi,\bar{\gamma}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)\rangle_{p+2}+\langle\phi,\bar{D}(\cdot,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)\rangle_{p+2}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p+2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{\gamma}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\phi^{\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\right\rVert}_{p+2}

\displaystyle+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p+2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bar{D}(z,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))[\cdot]\phi^{\prime\prime}(z)\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dz)\right\rVert}_{p+2}\text{by Cauchy Schwarz}

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl\{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{p+2}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p+2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{2}+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{p+2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}_{3}\biggr\}\text{ by Lemma \ref{lemma:KurtzAppendixAnalogues} and Assumption \ref{assumption:limitingcoefficientsregularity}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert}^{2}_{p+2}

\displaystyle=C\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}^{2}_{-(p+2)}.

The proof follows in the same way if we replace
w
with
r
.
∎

Proposition 8.8
.

Under Assumption
A13)
, for any
(Z,Q)
and
(\tilde{Z},Q)
such that
Q\in P^{*}(Z)
and
Q\in P^{*}(\tilde{Z})
,
Z=\tilde{Z}
as elements of
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-w})
. If we assume
A’13)
instead of
A13)
,
Z=\tilde{Z}
as elements of
C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-r})
.

Proof.

Consider
\eta=Z-\tilde{Z}
. Then by virtue of
(

P
enumi

1)
in the definition of
P^{*}
,
\eta
almost surely satisfies

\displaystyle\langle\eta_{t},\phi\rangle=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\eta_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi(\cdot)\rangle ds

for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{w}
. Let
\{\phi_{j}^{w+2}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be an orthonormal basis for
\mathcal{S}_{-(w+2)}
. By chain rule, we have

\displaystyle\langle\eta_{t},\phi_{j}^{w+2}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\eta_{s},\phi_{j}^{w+2}\rangle\langle\eta_{s},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}\phi_{j}^{w+2}(\cdot)\rangle ds.

Summing through
j
, we have using Parseval’s identity, Riesz representation theorem, and linearity of
\eta_{s}
and
\bar{L}_{\mathcal{L}(X_{s})}
that

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\eta(t)\right\rVert}_{-(w+2)}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{t}\langle\eta(s),\bar{L}^{*}_{\mathcal{L}(s)}\eta(s)ds\rangle_{-(w+2)}ds\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\eta(s)\right\rVert}_{-(w+2)}ds\text{ by Lemma \ref{lemma:4.32BW}}

so by Gronwall’s inequality,
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\eta(t)\right\rVert}_{-(w+2)}=0,\forall t\in[0,T]
, so
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\eta(t)\right\rVert}_{-w}=0,\forall t\in[0,T]
, and hence
Z=\tilde{Z}
.
The proof follows in the same way if we replace
w
with
r
.
∎

Remark 8.9
.

By
(

P
enumi

3)
and
(

P
enumi

4)
in the definition of
P^{*}
, we have that for any
Q\in P^{*}(Z)
that disintegrating
Q(dx,dy,dz,ds)=\kappa(dz;x,y,s)\lambda(dy;x,s)Q_{(1,4)}(dx,ds)
,
\lambda(dy;x,s)=\pi(dy;x,\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))
and
Q_{(1,4)}(dx,ds)=\mathcal{L}(X_{s})(dx)ds=\nu_{\mathcal{L}(X_{\cdot})}(dx,ds)
. Thus any
Q,\tilde{Q}\in P^{*}(Z)
only differentiate in their control stochastic kernels,
\kappa(dz;x,y,s)
and
\tilde{\kappa}(dz;x,y,s)
. These are, of course, entirely determined by the choice of controls in the construction of
Q^{N}
. In other words, keeping in mind the result of Proposition
8.4
, the choice of controls in the prelimit system (
55
) determine uniquely the limit in distribution of
\tilde{Z}^{N}.

9.
Laplace principle Lower Bound

We now can prove the Laplace principle Lower Bound (
30
).

Proposition 9.1
.

Under assumptions
A1)
-
A13)
, Equation (
30
) holds.

Proof.

Take
\tau\geq w
, with
w
as in Equation (
6
),
F\in C_{b}(C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-\tau}))
and
\eta>0
. By Equation (
51
) there exists
\{\tilde{u}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
N
,

\displaystyle-a^{2}(N)\log\mathbb{E}\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{a^{2}(N)}F(Z^{N})\biggr)\geq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}ds+F(\tilde{Z}^{N})\biggr]-\eta.

Where
\tilde{Z}^{N}
is as in Equation (
54
) and is controlled by
\{\tilde{u}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
. Then letting
Q^{N}
be as in Equation
(
56
)
with this choice of controls (recalling that we can assume the almost-sure bound (
53
) on the controls by the argument found in Theorem 4.4 of
[
2
]
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{T}\left(|\tilde{u}^{N,1}_{i}(s)|^{2}+|\tilde{u}^{N,2}_{i}(s)|^{2}\right)ds+F(\tilde{Z}^{N})\biggr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q^{N}(dxdydzds)+F(\tilde{Z}^{N})\biggr]

so by the version of Fatou’s lemma from Theorem A.3.12 in
[
13
]
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}-a^{2}(N)\log\mathbb{E}\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{a^{2}(N)}F(Z^{N})\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q^{N}(dxdydzds)+F(\tilde{Z}^{N})\biggr]-\eta

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q^{N}(dxdydzds)+F(\tilde{Z}^{N})\biggr]-\eta

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q(dxdydzds)+F(Z)\biggr]-\eta

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{Z\in C([0,T];\mathcal{S}_{-m})}\biggl\{\inf_{Q\in P^{*}(Z)}\biggl\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]}\left(|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}\right)Q(dxdydzds)\biggr\}+F(Z)\biggr\}-\eta
